successful sample executions does not exclude an omitted boundary or an incorrect condition
between the samples.
The selected example now has a stronger, exact connection to the declared accounting methods.
It proceeds in three stages: establish each method’s equations, substitute those equations
through the dated sequence, and divide the rate domain wherever a condition changes. This
division concerns computational work. It does not assert that a company, a manager or an
observer learns which historical explanation is true.
17.14.1
Compare the method with an equation, not another sample
An affine monetary expression is a constant plus a weighted sum of quantities. For this example,
those quantities include account balances, accrued and paid coupons, event amounts and the
supplied parameters I1, I2, R. Write an expression as
f(x) = c +
X
j
ajxj,
(17.17)
where each coefficient and the constant are exact rationals. The accounting language expresses
these calculations through constants, references, addition and subtraction. A constant con-
tributes to c; a reference contributes one coefficient; addition and subtraction combine the
coefficient lists. Induction on these four constructions shows that the resulting list represents
precisely the original expression. Two identical lists therefore give the same amount for every
known typed valuation, rather than merely for the values tested in a run.
For instance, the combined borrowing and payment changes cash by 3/4−It. The corresponding
coefficient of the due coupon is −1 and the constant is 3/4. Doubling both interest expense
and the credit to interest payable would still balance the journal. It would change these
independently specified account relations and fail the comparison. Balance is a necessary
accounting constraint, but it cannot establish that the right transaction was recorded.
The comparison also preserves the conditions on the transaction. A numerical comparison
is represented by its exact left-minus-right affine expression and its comparison sign. The
facility trigger It > 11/2 retains a strict sign; replacing it by It ≥11/2 fails even if a chosen
set of sample rates misses equality. Boolean conditions retain their operands, and memory
assignments retain their source and destination. The same check covers cash-flow origins: a
borrowing receipt, fee and interest payment cannot be replaced by one net cash movement.
Parameters enter through a separate selected template.
Its coupon and receipt amounts,
contract mode and facility right become ordinary typed literals before execution by the common
runtime. Exact placement checks bind the parameters to the opening state, event inputs and
selected-amount conditions. The surrounding method records retain their account maps, units,
dates, schemas, declared dependencies and refusal policies. This is a certificate for the inspected
selected profile. It does not extend the runtime to arbitrary symbolic programs or prove an
unrestricted compiler correct.
17.14.2
A correct event still needs a correct predecessor
Local agreement leaves a temporal question. When a method reads the payable or the facility
balance, does it receive the state produced by the preceding accepted event? Substitution
answers that question for the selected sequence. Every posting and memory assignment reads
the same pre-event state; the resulting changes become visible together. The next event receives
the complete new state, including accrued and paid coupon history.

The second accrual changes expense and the payable, leaving this cash unchanged. Substituting

facility liability while leaving the used-facility flag true. Omitting that memory would admit a
second draw which the contract in this example forbids.
The checked substitution compares all signed accounts, memory fields, gross flows and conditions
with independent complete formulas. It includes both refusal positions. A first refusal leaves the
first coupon accrued; a second refusal retains the accepted first-date events, receipt, repayment
and second accrual. It does not replace the final payable by zero merely because the proposed
payment appeared in the command sequence.
At this stage a branch can be written down even when no rate makes it feasible. For example,
the formal second-draw branch can be constructed after a first draw, but its unused-facility
condition is false. Distinguishing an algebraically described branch from a possible history is
the task of the next stage.
17.14.3
Why an empty branch needs a proof
Within each rate atom the capped coupon is constant or linear in the corresponding rate. A
point atom uses its exact constant coupon. Substituting those pieces into the conditions gives
inequalities in two variables, r1 and r2. Each comparison divides the current region into the
points satisfying it and the points satisfying its exact negation. For f ≤0, the other child is
−f < 0; for f < 0, it is −f ≤0. Equality belongs to one child, never to both and never to
neither.
A discarded child carries a short arithmetic reason why its inequalities cannot hold together.
Suppose the child requires
aT
i r + ci ≤0,
i = 1, . . . , m,
(17.21)
with some signs possibly strict. Choose rational weights λi ≥0 that cancel both variable
coefficients:
X
i
λiai = 0.
(17.22)
Adding the weighted inequalities then requires P
i λici ≤0. If that constant is positive, the
requirement is impossible. A zero constant also proves impossibility when at least one strict
inequality receives positive weight: the sum would require 0 < 0. These implications follow
directly by multiplying and adding the original inequalities. The verifier checks the weights
against those original rows rather than trusting a solver’s declaration that a branch is empty.
The distinction matters even in one dimension. The pair x < 0 and −x ≤0 has no solution:
equal unit weights produce 0 < 0. Weakening the first inequality to x ≤0 admits x = 0. A
certificate that discards the strictness would incorrectly remove that boundary point.
The reference producer eliminates the two variables with exact rational arithmetic and retains
the weighted combinations it uses. A separate checker verifies each proposed contradiction. A
retained leaf instead carries a point satisfying every inequality, including every strict sign. That


PAGE 154
CHAPTER 17.
CONTINUOUS FAMILIES AND EXACT BOUNDS
132
point proves the leaf is nonempty. The affine method and sequence argument, together with the
predicates along the branch, establishes the financial relation throughout the leaf; the witness
alone does not.
17.14.4
What can now leave the unfinished-work list
For the declared two-date model, all 3,872 rate-region and instruction obligations have a checked
partition. The partition contains 918 nonempty accepted leaves and 2,960 nonempty refusal
leaves; 4,772 additional children are discarded with checked contradiction certificates. These
are computational counts, not probabilities. They depend on the selected atom refinement and
cannot be interpreted as the likelihood of repayment or default.
The saved proof archive is re-read before its jobs enter a new certified queue. Each entry binds
the source revision, clause, method and sequence certificates, domain, query and horizon. A
pending job leaves the queue only with the corresponding verified proof. Zero-budget and
interrupted JSON continuations retain the same final partition as uninterrupted processing.
Checking the saved proof registry costs work in addition to the budget for newly completed
jobs.
The earlier concrete checkpoint correctly kept these regions unresolved under its own evidence.
The new result supplies separate whole-region certificates; it does not reinterpret the earlier
witness as a proof. The distinction is useful beyond this example: completing a computation
should mean satisfying the evidence obligation attached to that job, not merely reaching its
last instruction.
This result concerns known exact values, the supplied coupon and once-only facility terms, two
bounded rates and the selected event sequence. It leaves a further decision problem. An actor
can choose differently only when the actor’s information distinguishes the relevant histories.
Consequently, a list of financially feasible region-specific actions is not yet an admissible policy.
Section 17.15 binds these leaves to the supplied observation map and checks decisions that must
agree across indistinguishable histories. Section 17.16 supplies a conditional source-revision and
actor-access binding for the selected public-document profile; actual company evidence and
broader information structures remain separate requirements. Higher-dimensional and nonlinear
relations, unknown-value semantics, actual accounting authority, numerical quantisation and
crash-durable recovery require additional evidence.4
17.15
A feasible action for each world is not yet a policy
Return to the first coupon, when cash is 5.5 and the once-only facility is available. Two
companies can produce the same coarse observation that the market rate is high. In one, a
fixed coupon is 5; in the other, the floating coupon at the cap is 6. Paying is feasible for the
first company, while drawing is barred by the strict trigger. The second company cannot pay
from existing cash and needs the facility. If the manager sees only the coarse high signal, the
two individually feasible instructions do not form a decision rule the manager can follow. The
rule asks for information that the manager does not have.
This is the difference between a financially possible action and an information-feasible policy.

The condition gives one decision to each information cell. It does not require that the underlying

17.15.1
Refining an observation without inventing knowledge
The selected first coarse signal reports whether r1 ≤0.04. The refined signal adds whether
the first coupon exceeds 5.5. In the pair above, refinement distinguishes the two required first
instructions. It still does not reveal the facility right or generally identify a persistent coupon
type. At the second date the manager remembers the first signal and instruction, then observes
whether the second coupon crosses the trigger and whether the cash then available covers it.
The receipt hypothesis is held fixed for each comparison.
A financial region need not have a single observation label. For example, the condition that
drawing plus cash covers a coupon is weaker than the condition that existing cash alone covers it.
A region on which a draw is financially possible can therefore straddle the manager’s ordinary
cash-coverage observation. The checked construction splits that region on the cash-coverage
inequality and its exact complement. Empty children carry the contradiction proofs of the
preceding section; nonempty children retain their symbolic financial outcomes. Whole-atom
proofs likewise establish that the first low/high signal and each coupon-trigger signal are constant
where they are used. The observation is thus valid throughout the cell, rather than assigned
from one representative rate.
This timing is consequential. The first decision does not receive the second rate or a later cash
balance. The second decision uses cash before its proposed payment. Using terminal cash would
let the result of an action determine what the manager supposedly observed before choosing it.
Including the hidden facility right in the signal would change the information problem, even if
every resulting journal still balanced.
17.15.2
Which instructions survive every indistinguishable history?
For each possible first decision rule, retain every history consistent with that rule. A first-date
refusal rejects the rule. At each reachable second-date information cell, intersect the allowed
instructions across the retained histories. An empty intersection means there is no action that
works in every history the manager still considers possible. Otherwise choose one instruction
from each cell’s intersection. Cells that cannot be reached under the first rule need no arbitrary
decision in this comparison.
An independent formulation expresses the same condition as Boolean restrictions. Let Xo say
whether the first instruction at signal o is draw, and Yh the second instruction at remembered
history h. A certified first-date refusal under instruction a forbids Xo = a. A certified second-
date refusal under first instruction a and second instruction b forbids their joint selection:
(Xo ̸= a) ∨(Yh ̸= b).
(17.24)
Enumerating the satisfying assignments and retaining only reachable second-date choices gives
the complete policy set. This independently authored constraint calculation agrees with the
action-intersection calculation and with the earlier finite-policy reference. Agreement concerns
whole policies, not only the number found.


PAGE 156
CHAPTER 17.
CONTINUOUS FAMILIES AND EXACT BOUNDS
134
Across the eight supplied problems—receipts 6 or 7, coarse or refined observations, and committed
or mixed facility rights—only the receipt-7, refined-observation, committed-right problem has
robustly feasible policies. It has four. The first instruction is fixed by the refined trigger. After
a first draw, the exhausted facility cannot be used again and the second instruction must be
pay. After a first pay, two reachable second-date cells allow either pay or draw, giving 2 × 2 = 4
reachable policies. The model supplies no preference or objective that ranks them.
The failures have different causes. Coarse information combines incompatible first instructions.
A family containing an unavailable facility includes a high-coupon world with no feasible first
instruction. Receipt 6 leaves the double-high path short after repayment, despite knowing the
trigger and having the original right. Removing such a world or ignoring its refused branch
changes the robust question; it does not solve it.
These results concern the explicit family of possible histories and the supplied observation maps.
They assign no probabilities to that family, and a feasible robust policy is not a forecast or an
optimal policy. Applying the calculation to an actual manager next requires evidence about
source revisions, access times and observations. That evidence must preserve what was available
when a decision was made rather than replacing it with a later corrected account.5
17.16
Which source could this actor use at this date?
The four feasible policies in the preceding example depend on two supplied interpretations:
which receipt and facility-right family is possible, and which signals the decision maker observes.
A revised document can change either interpretation. It can also leave both interpretations
unchanged while correcting the evidence supporting them. The simulator needs to distinguish
those cases before it decides which calculation to repeat.
Consider a correction released on 3 January to a document used for a decision on 2 January. The
correction may improve a retrospective account of the company, but it cannot be inserted into
what the actor knew on 2 January merely because the simulator downloads it later. Conversely,
a document may have been available to the actor on 1 January but entered the simulator on 3
January and passed internal review on 4 January. On 4 January the system can reconstruct the
earlier information set if the retained evidence supports that access. It has not established that
the reconstruction itself was run on 2 January.
These are distinct dates with distinct subjects. Publication concerns the document’s release.
Access concerns a particular actor. Ingestion and review concern the information system.
Effective time concerns the economic period or event to which the interpretation applies. A
publication timestamp alone does not establish personal access; a download timestamp does
not establish either the original publication or the document’s economic effect.
17.16.1
Select revisions before selecting the policy problem
The selected construction uses an immutable record for each supplied interpretation. The record
identifies the source bytes, the interpretation’s subject, the previous revision it replaces, its
effective time, and separate observations of publication, access, ingestion and review. The caller
supplies the evidence accepted for these timing observations. Hashes preserve the identity of
those bytes and observations; they do not establish their truth.
5The complete observation refinements, whole-atom proofs, two policy formulations, source locks and eight com-
plete comparisons are retained in data/accounting-master/certified-observation-policy-20261026/attempt-01/.
The reference checks all 3,872 saved financial jobs, with 1,766 complementary observation refinements. These are
exact calculations for the selected synthetic information structure.


PAGE 157
CHAPTER 17.
CONTINUOUS FAMILIES AND EXACT BOUNDS
135
For a request, retain revisions that are effective for the chosen economic event, available to the
actor by the decision time, and ingested and reviewed by the system’s reconstruction cutoff.
Known later revisions are excluded from that earlier view. A missing relevant time leaves the
selection unresolved. Within a subject, select a revision only if it is the unique remaining head
of its replacement chain. Two incomparable available corrections preserve a conflict. Retraction
of a required interpretation leaves a missing input rather than silently restoring its earlier
assertion.
This procedure is deliberately restricted to a declared source scope. An unrelated management
note cannot change the coupon interpretation merely because it appears in the same registry.
A relevant correction must be inspected again on each request, however. Comparing a cached
view identifier without checking for newly available revisions could miss exactly the change that
should invalidate the result.
In the reference example, one required subject supplies receipt 6 or 7 and the mixed or committed
facility-right family; the other supplies the coarse or refined observation scheme. These choices
map to the eight previously certified problems. They preserve the same possible persistent
contract types, complete financial rows and rejected actions within the selected family. No
convenient prior or likelihood is introduced to make a source selector callable. The question
remains whether one policy works in every history the actor cannot distinguish.
The timing checks reuse the existing source and policy-information mechanisms. The selected
adapter accepts explicit whole-second UTC instants and public-document evidence with a
separate supplied actor-access observation. It refuses finer or incomplete clock representations
instead of rounding them into eligibility.
Section 17.17 extends this selected reference to
exact clock ranges and an explicit private-delivery route; broader information practices remain
separate. An unresolved access record is therefore a substantive result about this request, not
an instruction to substitute the publication date.
17.16.2
Reuse a calculation without reusing an obsolete source binding
Suppose a replacement document supports exactly the same receipt, facility-right family and
observation scheme. The set of robust policies can remain unchanged. Nevertheless, a report
that still cites the replaced source would give the wrong explanation of which evidence supported
the current request. Numerical equality and evidential identity answer different questions.
Let F denote the retained financial relations and certified rows, s the selected receipt and
facility family, and o the observation scheme. Write the complete reachable policy calculation as
P(F, s, o).
(17.25)
Its numerical inputs do not include a document’s filename or the system’s ingestion date. Those
records determine whether the interpretation (s, o) is available for the actor and request. The
reported result therefore needs both P(F, s, o) and the source, revision, actor and cutoff records
that justify using these inputs.
The checked dependency relation makes this separation explicit. Certified financial evidence
supports the chosen financial scenario; that scenario and the observation scheme support the
policy set. Separate source bindings attach the selected financial and observation interpretations
to their revision histories. The final actor binding joins the numerical policy set to both source
bindings and to the decision, reconstruction and effective cutoffs.
A same-meaning correction changes a source binding and the final actor binding while preserving
the numerical policy set. A change from coarse to refined information preserves the financial
relations but requires a new observation and policy calculation. A changed receipt or facility


PAGE 158
CHAPTER 17.
CONTINUOUS FAMILIES AND EXACT BOUNDS
136
family changes the financial scenario and its dependent policy problem. Changing only the actor
or cutoff requires a new actor binding even when the selected interpretations and numerical
answer happen to be identical. A correction known to arrive after the old cutoff changes none
of that earlier view’s selected dependencies.
Reuse is justified by those dependencies, not by observing that the new and old policy counts
coincide. The reference compares the entire reused policy set and its complete world-row
population with a fresh calculation. It also requires the saved inputs and method bytes to
match expected identities supplied outside the transported bundle. Removed or duplicated
rows, changed hidden rights or signals, altered policy references and rewritten prior results are
rejected. Recomputing an inner checksum after changing a saved result does not make it the
previously accepted result.
The scope remains conditional. The caller must establish which source and timing evidence is
accepted, and a complete history family for an actual company may require many more subjects
than these two. The result shows how revision-sensitive selection can coexist with numerical
reuse while preserving the robust question. It establishes neither authenticated storage nor
crash recovery, and the small exact comparison supplies no performance ranking.6
17.17
A date range can leave several information views possible
A filing marked as published on 2 January does not establish whether a manager had read it at
the beginning of that day. Nor does a correction received within a narrow interval around a
decision establish whether the original or corrected interpretation was available for that decision.
These are questions about the order of events. Assigning midnight or the interval’s midpoint
would settle them by assumption.
The selected successor retains that uncertainty explicitly. An exact timestamp is a point; a
calendar day in UTC is a half-open interval from its midnight to the next midnight. A supplied
time range has the same half-open convention. An unknown access time remains unknown.
Exact rational seconds preserve differences smaller than a microsecond and equivalent fixed-offset
spellings without passing through a floating-point or microsecond clock.
If the access time lies in [a, b), a decision at b is certainly after access. A decision at a can
coincide with access or precede it: the lower endpoint belongs to the interval. The distinction
disappears if an implementation replaces the interval with a single representative time. It also
disappears if a strict upper bound is inadvertently changed to a closed one.
17.17.1
Availability depends on the route and on joint chronology
A public-document retrieval and a private delivery impose different conditions. In the first
route the source must have been released publicly before the claimed access. In the second,
an actor-addressed delivery can establish access before any public release. The private route
neither invents a public timestamp nor makes the document available to other actors. Both
routes still require evidence about that actor’s access; publication alone cannot supply it.
The earlier whole-second reference did not distinguish these routes. One of its retained cases
places actor access before a later public release.
Under the new, explicit public-retrieval
premise, that chronology is inconsistent. It can be meaningful under a private-delivery premise.

Preserving the difference is necessary: changing the access mechanism changes the question
rather than improving timestamp precision alone.
Clocks are also dependent. Authorship precedes ingestion, ingestion precedes review, and
authorship precedes access. Public retrieval additionally follows publication. The selected
revision profile requires nondecreasing authorship, ingestion, review and same-actor access along
a replacement chain, while permitting retrospective effective dates. These are declared premises
of this bounded source model. A document practice that violates one of them requires another
profile; a corrected economic effective date is not forced forward merely to preserve a convenient
time ordering.
An unknown clock need not make every conclusion unknown. If a private access time is supplied
before the decision and the document was necessarily authored before that access, the chronology
can establish the relevant authorship inequality without identifying its timestamp. The result
must retain the unknown-time marker. Logical implication from other premises does not turn
an unobserved time into an observed fact.
17.17.2
Partition clock possibilities without enumerating timestamps
Let xe be the time of event e, measured in exact UTC seconds on the retained calendar axis.
Point and interval observations, chronology and cutoff conditions become difference inequalities.
A non-strict edge represents
xv −xu ≤c,
(17.26)
with a strict variant when the right endpoint is excluded. A fixed origin converts an absolute
bound into the same form. The source identities, actors, access routes and original precision
remain attached to these mathematical restrictions; the inequalities alone do not establish the
source facts.
For example, publication before access contributes xpublication ≤xaccess, while access by the
decision contributes xaccess ≤tdecision. Keeping both inequalities in one system prevents the
calculation from accepting a combination that separate per-clock classifications would mistakenly
permit.
For each revision, eligibility is a conjunction of its required actor, system and effective-time
guards, g1, . . . , gm. The complementary cases can be written as a disjoint first-failure partition:
¬(g1 ∧· · · ∧gm) = ¬g1 ∨(g1 ∧¬g2) ∨· · · ∨(g1 ∧· · · ∧gm−1 ∧¬gm).
(17.27)
Each branch preserves the earlier satisfied guards. Repeating the construction for the relevant
revisions covers every clock assignment admitted by the original ranges and chronology. Eligibility
and the selected revision heads are constant within each surviving branch. The procedure
therefore represents families of timestamps symbolically; it does not need an explicit tree point
for every possible instant.
An empty branch requires a checked contradiction. Summing original difference inequalities
around a cycle cancels all clock variables. A negative right-hand sum is impossible; a zero sum
is also impossible if at least one inequality in the sum is strict. A nonempty branch instead
carries exact rational clock values satisfying every original inequality. The checker verifies those
values directly and reconstructs every complementary branch before accepting the partition.
The witness establishes nonemptiness, while the guards and complete partition establish the
scope of the conclusion. A few feasible timestamp examples would establish neither exhaustive
coverage nor the absence of an omitted information view. Conversely, reaching the computation
budget leaves an unfinished temporal family. The selected reference then reports incompleteness
and does not present the already examined views as the full set.

17.17.3
Different timing views can support different policies
Return to receipt 7 and the committed facility. Suppose a correction changes the available
observation scheme from coarse to refined, but the actor’s access interval straddles the first
decision. There are two remaining timing views. In the view where the correction has not yet
arrived, the coarse signal leaves incompatible first instructions in the same information cell
and no robust policy is feasible. In the view where it has arrived, the refined signal supports
the four previously derived policies. The complete financial row population is retained in both
calculations.
The timing evidence supplies no probability for either view. Selecting the four-policy answer
would discard the other possibility.
Reporting the two answers separately is therefore a
substantive conclusion.
Constructing one decision rule that is admissible when even the
observation mechanism is uncertain requires a common specification of what the actor sees and
how actions are represented across those mechanisms; a naive intersection of differently labelled
policy tables is insufficient.
The exact comparison verifies interval boundaries, equivalent offsets, unknown and untrusted
clocks, public and private access, correlated correction times, conflicting revisions and retractions.
The saved partitions retain unresolved source outcomes as well as selected ones. Their scope is
the supplied chronology and access model, not an empirical statement about how a real company
releases information. General revision practices, family-level dependency reuse, authenticated
evidence and durable recovery remain separate questions.7

PAGE 161
Chapter 18
Reference computations that
distinguish a useful approximation
from a wrong answer
A method can be tested most clearly when a small version of its target is known exactly. This
chapter constructs such targets for cash bounds, breach probabilities, policy comparison and
residual minimization. The calculations are analytic reference cases. They are not a benchmark
ranking and do not supply empirical evidence about a real company. Their role is to expose
errors that a large simulation can conceal behind apparently stable averages.
18.1
An exact distribution from a declared prior
Return to cash C = 90−2α, and now deliberately assume α is uniform on [0, 1]. This assumption
is not inferred from the financial statements. It creates a transparent probabilistic reference.
Cash is uniform on [88, 90], with mean 89 and variance 4/12 = 1/3.
For a covenant requiring cash at least 89.4, breach occurs when α > 0.3. Its probability is 0.7.
The expected cash conditional on breach is the mean of the interval [88, 89.4), namely 88.7.
These quantities answer different questions: unconditional mean, breach frequency and severity
conditional on breach.
A simulation with the right mean but the wrong breach probability is not accurate for covenant
analysis. For example, a two-point approximation placing equal mass at 88 and 90 has mean 89
but breach probability one half. Matching the first moment does not preserve threshold events.
The exact distribution also gives quantiles. Under the usual left-continuous generalized-inverse
convention, the p-quantile is 88 + 2p for 0 < p < 1, with endpoint values defined by continuous
extension. The convention should be explicit when discrete mass points are introduced, because
quantile definitions can differ at jumps.
18.2
A discrete grid with known bias
Approximate α by equally weighted midpoints (i −1/2)/n, for i = 1, . . . , n. The mean of the
grid remains one half, so mean cash is exactly 89 for every n. Breach frequency is the fraction
of midpoints above 0.3. For n = 4, midpoints are 0.125, 0.375, 0.625 and 0.875, giving breach
probability 0.75.
139


PAGE 162
CHAPTER 18.
REFERENCE COMPUTATIONS
140
The mean check passes perfectly while the breach estimate differs from 0.7. Increasing n
improves the discretization in this example, but its error depends on the threshold’s location
relative to the grid. A convergence study should examine the queried event, not only smooth
moments.
If the threshold lies exactly on a grid point, strict versus inclusive inequality changes the finite
approximation. The continuous reference assigns zero probability to one point, but a discrete
approximation assigns positive mass. This is a concrete reason to preserve boundary semantics
during discretization.
A deterministic grid is not an independent random sample. Standard Monte Carlo standard
errors do not describe its quadrature error. Its uncertainty should be assessed through analytic
bounds or refinement, not by treating grid points as independent observations.
18.3
A nonlinear guard with a narrow region
Let α remain uniform on [0, 1], but suppose a special covenant fails only when |α −0.5| < 0.01.
The exact probability is 0.02. A coarse endpoint or midpoint grid can miss this region entirely.
A random sample of size 100 has probability 0.98100, about 0.133, of observing no failures.
A deterministic grid can have either zero or several hits depending on alignment. The two
methods fail for different reasons: sampling variation versus systematic discretization geometry.
A query-aware method should detect the guard and refine around its roots. If the guard is
known analytically, solving its boundary is more efficient than hoping random paths discover it.
Sampling remains useful when the region is complex, but the model should exploit available
structure.
The example also warns against dropping a region because its volume is small. A two per cent
event can dominate expected loss if severity is large. Probability and consequence must be
evaluated together.
18.4
A rare-loss mean
Suppose loss is zero with probability 0.999 and 1,000 with probability 0.001. Expected loss is 1,
while the most common observed loss is zero. In 100 independent draws, the chance of no loss
is 0.999100, about 0.905. Most such short runs report a sample mean of zero.
The variance is 0.001(10002) −12 = 999. The standard error of the sample mean at n = 100 is
p
999/100, about 3.16. A single observed mean cannot support a precise estimate. A normal
approximation is also questionable in a sample with very few tail events.
This target can test whether a simulator reports uncertainty honestly. A zero-event run should
not declare zero risk. A rare-event method should be checked against the known probability
and mean, including its weighting and interval procedure.
The example does not establish how rare real defaults are. Its parameters are chosen to make
sampling failure visible. A real application needs an admitted event law and severity model,
with model uncertainty separate from Monte Carlo error.


PAGE 163
CHAPTER 18.
REFERENCE COMPUTATIONS
141
18.5
Importance sampling on the rare event
For the same two-state target, choose a proposal that samples the loss state with probability 0.1
instead of 0.001. The target-to-proposal weight is 0.01 in the loss state and 0.999/0.9 = 1.11 in
the zero state. The ordinary weighted loss contribution is therefore 10 in the loss state and zero
otherwise.
Its expectation under the proposal is 0.1(10) = 1, the correct target mean. Its variance is
0.1(102) −1 = 9, much smaller than 999 in this constructed example. The reduction follows
because the proposal deliberately visits the only important rare state.
This is a mathematical comparison for a known finite distribution, not a general performance
claim for importance sampling. In a high-dimensional model, a poor proposal can create extreme
weights or miss support. A proposal tuned to one failure mode may neglect another. The finite
case provides a correctness test for the weighting formula.
If the implementation averages the unweighted sampled loss, it obtains expectation 100 under
the proposal, not target expectation 1. If it reports the proportion of sampled defaults as the
target probability, it reports 0.1 instead of 0.001. These large errors can look stable as sample
size grows because the wrong quantity is being estimated accurately.
18.6
Paired policy differences
Suppose two policies have costs YA = Z + 2 and YB = Z + 1, where Z is a common random
operating cost. The true difference YA −YB is exactly 1 in every world. Using the same draw
of Z gives zero sampling variance for the difference. Independent draws add unnecessary noise.
This is an ideal illustration of common random numbers. In a real policy comparison, the
policies can change transition probabilities or event timing. The coupling must preserve each
policy’s correct marginal distribution. Reusing a random seed alone does not guarantee that
corresponding random draws represent the same economic shock after control flow diverges.
A stable shock identity can help: demand innovation for a named period and customer should
remain associated with that economic event rather than the next call to a global random-number
generator. Otherwise adding a harmless diagnostic draw can change all subsequent scenarios.
The paired comparison should also hold the initial history fixed. Subtracting policy A on one
reconstructed company from policy B on another confounds policy and history. If the goal is a
range of policy differences, optimize the paired difference over the same family.
18.7
A sampled minimum and a true minimum
Let C(α) = 90 −2α on [0, 1]. The true minimum is 88 at α = 1. A sample whose largest value
is 0.9 has minimum cash 88.2. This is an upper bound on the true minimum because the sample
is a subset of the family.
If a payment requirement is 88.1, the sampled minimum suggests no sampled failure while
the true family contains failure. An optimization or analytic monotonicity argument identifies
it immediately. More random sampling may eventually approach the endpoint, but no finite
sample alone proves the exact bound.
Conversely, a relaxed outer model allowing α ∈[0, 1.1] gives minimum cash 87.8. That is a
lower bound on the original minimum, but its witness is not a feasible original company. Inner
and outer methods can bracket the answer. The bracket’s width, not a single attractive number,


PAGE 164
CHAPTER 18.
REFERENCE COMPUTATIONS
142
describes unresolved computation.
18.8
A local optimizer misses another basin
Consider an illustrative cash function C(x) = (x2 −1)2 + 0.1x on [−2, 2]. It has regions near
both negative and positive one where cash is relatively low. A local optimizer initialized near
one basin may not find the global minimum.
The function is a numerical diagnostic, not a proposed cash-flow mechanism. Its purpose is to
test whether a method labels a local result honestly. A global claim requires exhaustive interval
bounds, verified comparison of all stationary and boundary candidates, or another suitable
certificate.
In a financial model, analogous nonconvexity can arise from fixed issuance fees, default options,
tax thresholds or discrete investment. Repeated random starts can find more candidates but do
not automatically prove completeness. The result should distinguish found local solutions from
a verified global bound.
A benchmark should include such a case only if the target profile admits nonconvexity. Requiring
a linear solver to solve it would be an unfair capability test. Conversely, describing a general
solver while testing only convex examples leaves a material gap.
18.9
Residual scaling can alter a compromise
Suppose an approximation cannot satisfy two residuals simultaneously: r1(θ) = θ and r2(θ) =
θ −1. Minimizing r2
1 + r2
2 gives θ = 1/2. If the first residual is multiplied by 100 because
currency units changed, minimizing 10000θ2 + (θ −1)2 gives θ = 1/10001.
The different result is caused by weighting, not new economic information. In a well-specified
exact system with sufficient capacity, both residuals may be zero and scaling would not change
the exact solution. Under approximation, inconsistency or finite optimization, scaling affects
the compromise.
This diagnostic motivates reporting component residuals in original units and testing unit
invariance. A normalized aggregate loss can be useful for training, but it should not hide a cash
pricing error or constraint violation. A single loss threshold needs an economic interpretation.
18.10
Expected residual versus mean squared contribution
The earlier example r = θ−1+θε with mean-zero unit-variance innovation gives desired squared
expected residual (θ −1)2 and single-draw squared objective (θ −1)2 + θ2. Their minimizers
are one and one half.
A reference implementation can evaluate both objectives analytically and by Monte Carlo. It
should verify that independent paired draws estimate the former expectation and identical
draws estimate the latter. The test should compare the target functions, not merely whether
training loss decreases.
A gradient check can add another layer. The desired derivative is 2(θ−1); the other derivative is
4θ −2. At θ = 1/2, the second vanishes while the first is negative one. Automatic differentiation
can return the correct derivative of the wrong implemented objective.
This case is particularly relevant to learned equilibrium conditions because it separates stochastic


PAGE 165
CHAPTER 18.
REFERENCE COMPUTATIONS
143
estimation from target definition. Increasing batch size makes the wrong objective estimate
more precise; it does not repair the missing expectation structure.
18.11
A boundary optimum
Minimize (a + 1)2 subject to a ≥0. The answer is a = 0, objective 1. An interior first-order
condition gives a = −1, objective zero, which is infeasible. A constrained solver should return
the boundary with the appropriate multiplier.
A penalty objective (a + 1)2 + ρ min(a, 0)2 has a negative optimum for every finite positive ρ,
namely a = −1/(1 + ρ). The violation shrinks as the penalty grows but is not exactly zero.
Reporting the penalized optimum as a feasible original solution without checking the constraint
is wrong.
Penalty methods can be useful algorithms. Their residual feasibility must be assessed against
the original constraint and the required tolerance. A small violation may be immaterial for
some economic quantities and decisive when it represents issuing more shares than authorized
or paying cash that does not exist.
18.12
Designing a fair method comparison
A comparison should use the same evidence family, objective, policy class, timing, terminal
condition and accuracy target. A faster method that omits a recovery term is not a faster
solution of the original pricing model. A method trained on a smaller domain should not be
compared as though it covers the full stress region.
The baseline ladder can include exact finite enumeration, a refined classical numerical method,
the plain proposed approximation and any enhanced version. Tuning budgets and stopping
criteria should be declared. A deliberately weak comparator can establish only a narrow
improvement over that comparator.
For stochastic methods, use replications and uncertainty analysis appropriate to the claim.
If several candidates pass hard validity screens but differences are not statistically resolved,
describe them as viable under current evidence. Runtime, tail error and objective values from a
few seeds remain descriptive unless the predeclared analysis supports ranking.
18.13
What to retain from a run
A run should preserve the model and data version, command, environment, seeds, hardware
mode, wall time, outputs and the plan defining the question. It should record which diagnostics
are validity vetoes, which trigger repair and which merely explain behavior.
The result note should identify whether a failure invalidated the harness, implementation, target
or candidate. A candidate failing a planned stress can motivate a repair phase. It does not
automatically reject the broader research direction. A broken harness, by contrast, prevents
interpreting the candidate comparison.
For the present manuscript, these are proposed experiments and exact reference calculations.
No stochastic ranking is claimed. The accompanying arithmetic script checks the deterministic
examples and records its scope. Future numerical runs should use these cases to establish
semantics before making claims about scalable performance.


PAGE 166
Chapter 19
What the literatures contribute, and
where their assumptions stop
The simulation problem crosses several literatures because it combines inference from incomplete
reports, accounting representation, dynamic corporate finance, sequential information and
computational approximation. No inspected source supplies the entire system. The useful
synthesis identifies the problem each source actually solves and the additional work needed for
AccDSL. A source’s notation or software can be valuable without becoming a universal model
of a disclosed company.
19.1
Economic events and accounting views
McCarthy’s REA model provides a useful architectural antecedent. Resources, events and
agents, linked through stock-flow, duality and control relations, organise economic activity
before particular accounting views are produced (McCarthy, 1982, pp. 560–570). Double entry
can be one external view. Claims can be derived from imbalances or retained as base records
when their uses warrant it. The paper also recognises the practical need for aggregation and
the fact that measurement and periodicity are not settled by a database schema (McCarthy,
1982, pp. 568–576). These distinctions support an event-and-contract representation with
journal views. They do not imply that disclosures identify a unique event history, or supply the
likelihood of observing a disclosure.
19.2
Inverse problems and incomplete information
Stuart (2010) develops Bayesian inverse problems with explicit forward models, priors and
analytical conditions. It is relevant because statements are observations generated by hidden
economic histories under a reporting map. The source does not reconstruct company journals
or justify a particular prior over contracts. Its contribution is a disciplined distinction between
the observation relation and the assumptions needed for inference.
Libkin (2014, 2016a) studies incomplete databases and answers across possible worlds. This
perspective fits the requirement to preserve alternatives without assigning probabilities. A
certain answer holds across admitted worlds; a possible answer has a witness. Translating that
logic to finance requires a domain of economic histories and valid accounting relations, not
merely null values in a table.
The two approaches are complementary. A feasible family expresses what has not been ruled
144


PAGE 167
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
145
out. A probability measure expresses relative weight under a model. A Bayesian posterior can
be computed on a family when priors and likelihoods are justified, but the family itself should
not be discarded when those assumptions are uncertain.
19.3
Set identification and prior sensitivity
Giacomini and Kitagawa (2021, Sections 2.1–2.3) separates parameters identified by the observ-
able distribution from remaining directions whose conditional priors are not revised by data.
The retained technical reading uses the April 2020 working-paper version associated with the
later publication. That version identity matters when citing equations and claims.
For company simulation, the immediate implication is conditional: first determine which hidden
features the observation model identifies. A long history of interest expense may leave fixed-
versus-floating terms unresolved when rates have not varied. Additional data of the same
uninformative kind need not eliminate the ambiguity.
The local history family is not automatically the identified set of the source’s statistical model.
Finite compatibility with reports is weaker than equality of full observable laws. The manuscript
therefore uses report-compatible histories unless the stronger identification argument has been
established. This avoids borrowing a technical term to imply more evidence than exists.
19.4
Model averaging
Hoeting et al. (1999) surveys Bayesian model averaging, in which uncertainty across models is
represented by posterior weights. This can be useful when alternative economic or observation
models are explicitly specified. It also makes clear that averaging requires model priors and
likelihoods, not branch counts.
The practical difficulty for AccDSL is defining model identity. Ten syntactic encodings of one
contract should not receive ten times the weight of a single encoding of another. A prior over
economic structures must be invariant to irrelevant representation choices or at least expose its
dependence.
Model averaging can also conceal a decision-relevant minority case if the report shows only a mean.
Conditional outcomes, tail measures and sensitivity to prior weights remain important. When
weights are not defensible, the unweighted family is the appropriate retained representation.
19.5
Causal scenarios
Pearl (2009, Sections 3.2–3.4) distinguishes observational conditioning, interventions and coun-
terfactual reasoning. This distinction matters whenever a financial scenario is described as
the effect of a shock. A model fitted to periods with high rates need not identify the effect of
changing rates while other mechanisms remain fixed.
AccDSL can support deterministic stress paths without claiming causal identification. Such a
run answers a conditional mechanical question. A causal claim additionally needs structural
equations, intervention semantics and assumptions about confounding and invariance.
Counterfactual comparisons require a coupling between factual and alternative worlds. Preserv-
ing the same hidden history and exogenous innovations is one modeled coupling. Independently
drawing a new company for each policy changes the comparison. The causal literature supplies
the conceptual distinction; the company-specific structure remains to be justified.


PAGE 168
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
146
19.6
Corporate microsimulation
Shahnazarian (2004) links financial stocks through accounting bridges and separately specifies
behavioral relationships for incorporated businesses. The inspected sections include stock
movements, investment, disposal, depreciation and tax-related distinctions.
The source’s
Swedish data and tax setting are not modern universal issuer defaults.
Its relevance is methodological: identities constrain movement while behavioral equations deter-
mine choices. A simulator can preserve that separation while using different data, accounting
rules and policies. Importing estimated coefficients without target-specific evidence would be
an unsupported transfer.
The source also illustrates why state includes more than current statement totals. Tax reserves,
capital and other accumulated quantities affect future responses. AccDSL’s state design should
identify such memory by mechanism rather than simply copying a fixed list of variables from
one model.
19.7
Accounting in system dynamics
Pierson (2020) develops accounting reporting within a system-dynamics model. Accrual, payment
and reporting accumulation are separate, and the retained author model exposes equations for
cash, receivables, payables and report timing. These are useful implementation concepts.
The source’s payables payment rule uses a delay approximation, and its financing and inventory
mechanisms have stated limits. A first-order payment delay is not an invoice-level due-date
schedule. Reporting values expressed as annualized rates must be converted before comparison
with period amounts.
The source therefore contributes a bridge between continuous simulation stocks and discrete
reports. It does not establish a complete instrument-level financing engine or empirical validity
for a particular company. AccDSL should reuse the separation of clocks while admitting more
detailed schedules when the query requires them.
19.8
Agent-based stock-flow consistency
Caiani et al. (2016) models heterogeneous agents, loans and capital vintages with an explicit
within-period sequence. The retained paper and selected official code show production, credit,
payments, taxes and distributions connected through counterpart balance changes. This supports
the importance of event ordering and financial counterparts.
The source begins from an artificial economy with its own initialization and calibration. That is
not a reconstruction of an observed issuer from disclosures. Its reported simulation properties
are author results, not experiments reproduced for AccDSL.
A particularly useful source-to-target warning concerns its operating-cash-flow label: the
inspected definition subtracts scheduled principal repayments. That quantity can be appropriate
for its credit-demand mechanism, but is not automatically the same as a financial-statement
operating cash-flow category. Definitions must travel with values across adapters.
Selected code inspection is also bounded. The retained implementation revision exposes event
switches, debt payment calculations and bankruptcy paths. It does not certify every formula,
the entire runtime or correspondence with the paper-era executable. Before reuse, zero-rate
schedules, timing, numerical domains and accounting adapters need targeted checks.


PAGE 169
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
147
19.9
Financial planning and simultaneous equations
The retained Gentry working paper studies strategic financial planning from management inputs
such as earnings-per-share growth, investment growth, leverage and financing costs (Gentry,
1974). It asks an inverse planning question about returns needed to achieve objectives. Its
managerial distributions and independence assumptions are part of that model, not facts about
every company.
The retained scan lacks a referenced derivation appendix in the inspected trailing pages, and
correspondence with the later journal version has not been established. The manuscript therefore
does not rely on an unchecked derivation as a validated algorithm. This is a source-specific
limit, not a reason to dismiss the planning approach.
Gentry and Hoque (1990) organizes cash-flow components and estimates simultaneous behavioral
equations. Its accounting reconciliation, definitions and behavioral relationships perform different
jobs. The retained reading identifies a normalization inconsistency in one displayed cash term
and records the local algebraic normalization explicitly. It does not silently claim a corrected
edition.
The reported sample fits do not establish out-of-sample intervention validity. Firm-level data,
a complete instrument matrix and code were not available for local replication. The useful
comparison is architectural: a simultaneous system can propose related annual amounts, while
a scenario engine still needs dated events, feasibility, information and accounting treatment.
19.10
Dynamic corporate finance
Strebulaev and Whited (2012) surveys dynamic corporate-finance models with investment,
financing frictions and default. The risky-debt section makes debt pricing and equity value
interdependent. This is essential for any model claiming endogenous financing terms.
The retained equations also illustrate why source inspection cannot stop at a citation. The
face/proceeds convention mismatch in equations (3.26)–(3.27) must be reconciled before imple-
mentation. A positive-recovery calculation exposes it; a no-default example can conceal it. The
present manuscript uses distinct cash and promise symbols and retains the source qualification.
Other simplifications require equally explicit transfer decisions. Signed net debt can collapse
cash and borrowing. Present-valued tax benefits are not actual tax receipts. Aggregate issuance
costs do not create legal share rights. A structural model can be internally useful under these
assumptions while remaining insufficient for a full accounting simulator.
19.11
Bank funding and adjustment
Halaj (2016) models portfolio choices under uncertain funding and constraints. Behn et al.
(2019) links bank behavior to capital and liquidity restrictions using its own supervisory-data
setting and structural assumptions. These sources show that funding is constrained behavior,
not an automatic balancing liability.
A corporate issuer does not inherit their bank-specific objective or regulatory measurements.
Their estimated parameters are not identified from public company statements merely be-
cause the balance-sheet labels resemble those of banks. AccDSL must distinguish accounting,
prudential and economic quantities.
The transferable design principle is a policy interface that receives state, information and


PAGE 170
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
148
constraints jointly. Funding availability can change under stress and affect asset decisions.
Applying that principle to another sector requires sector-specific rules and evidence, not a
relabeled bank equation.
19.12
Contract algorithms
The retained ACTUS technical specification separates schedules, state transitions and payoffs
(ACTUS Financial Research Foundation, 2026). This is a promising component boundary
for contractual cash flows. It does not supply operating behavior, accounting recognition,
consolidation or a justified future risk process.
The inspected specification and service code leave important adapter conventions to be resolved:
timezone mapping, midnight interpretation, same-date event order, day-count implementation,
currency scaling and risk observations. A service fallback to current wall-clock time plus a
horizon is incompatible with reproducible historical simulation unless overridden explicitly.
The selected service requests a particular core dependency but does not establish a fully
resolved, matched implementation and dictionary version. No runtime adoption follows from
the inspection alone. A conditional algebraic correspondence for a simple borrower loan is useful
evidence, but not complete executable conformance.
19.13
Sequential decisions under partial observation
Kaelbling et al. (1998) provides the belief-state formulation of planning under partial observation
with specified transition and observation probabilities. It explains how information history can
be summarized for control. The relevant sections were inspected in the retained author preprint,
including rendered pages because the copy is scanned.
AccDSL adds two complications. Some uncertainty is unweighted and cannot be represented
as a single probability belief without assumptions. Different actors can have different private
information. A public analyst’s belief is not necessarily the state on which management acts.
The POMDP formulation is therefore a method for an admitted probabilistic subproblem,
not the universal semantics of all company uncertainty. It is especially useful for testing
nonanticipativity, signal value and belief updates in finite reference cases.
19.14
Exact aggregation and knowledge compilation
Givan et al. (2003) gives equivalence and minimization results for MDPs, including policy-
preserving stochastic bisimulation. Its fixed-action counterexample explains why equal outputs
under preselected action sequences can be too weak.
AccDSL’s local extension preserves
observations and requested outputs as part of a joint successor law.
Darwiche and Marquis (2002) maps representational tradeoffs among compiled logical languages.
It supports sharing and query-aware structure while warning that succinctness and tractable
operations trade off. It does not prove that arbitrary financial histories can be compiled cheaply.
Together these sources support a careful answer to state explosion: share common structure,
preserve joint constraints and aggregate only under a scoped sufficiency condition. They do not
support the claim that replacing a tree with a graph eliminates exponential worst cases.


PAGE 171
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
149
19.15
Filtering and approximation
Doucet and Johansen (2008) explains particle filtering, smoothing, degeneracy and conditional
analytic integration in the retained tutorial version. Its technical treatment motivates weighted
particles and diagnostics when a state-space probability model is specified.
Unknown static contract parameters require additional care. Repeated resampling can remove
types without any evidence ruling them out. A high effective sample size after resampling does
not guarantee historical diversity. The retained feasible family should therefore remain available
alongside the particle approximation.
Dupačová et al. (2000, Sections 2–4) studies probability-metric scenario reduction in the retained
2000 preprint. Theorem 3.1 solves redistribution for a fixed subset; the subsequent subset-
selection algorithms are heuristics. The stability results depend on the stochastic program and
its regularity, while the reported electrical-load experiment measures distribution distances in
its own setting. It does not establish company default accuracy or preserve every adaptive
policy. The local derivations in Chapter 9 explain the resulting expected-output and policy
bounds, and the discontinuous-threshold counterexample shows their limits.
19.16
Dynamic ambiguity
Epstein and Schneider (2002) derives recursive multiple-priors preferences under stated axioms
and rectangularity relative to an information filtration. The definition, theorem and relevant
proof structure were inspected in the retained September 2002 preprint. The result concerns a
particular preference representation.
Its relevance is the danger of using stagewise worst-case recursion with an uncertainty set that
restricts cross-time dependence. Rectangular enlargement can change the admitted joint laws.
A fixed unknown contract cannot silently become an adversary choosing a new type each period.
AccDSL should offer commitment, original-set and recursive robust formulations only with their
semantics stated. The choice can alter policy and should remain visible. Dynamic consistency
is not a label that makes every robust calculation equivalent.
19.17
Learned economic solvers
Maliar et al. (2021) formulates dynamic economic equations for deep learning, with careful
distinctions among residual objectives and sampling constructions. Its definitions and algorithm
are relevant to the supplied lecture’s proposed solver. The double-sampling derivation in this
part shows why the target objective matters before training begins.
Duarte and Fonseca (2026) is a newer structural-estimation working paper with jointly solved
financing and value components, lagged-network training and local refinement. Its assumptions,
state and pricing conventions differ from the lecture model. Its numerical organization is a
candidate, not a verified AccDSL implementation or general global-optimality result.
Official code for these learned methods has not been comprehensively audited for this local inte-
gration. Consequently, the manuscript makes no code-equivalence, convergence or performance
promotion claim. Any implementation decision must add that inspection and a target-specific
numerical protocol.


PAGE 172
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
150
19.18
Disclosure selection and the observation likelihood
Imagine equal prior probabilities of a good and bad type. If good types disclose with probability
0.9 and bad types with probability 0.1, then disclosure raises the probability of a good type
to 0.9, while silence reduces it to 0.1. Treating a missing disclosure as no information would
retain 0.5 in both cases. The discrepancy comes from omitting the selection mechanism, not
from numerical approximation. These probabilities are an authored example, not estimates of
actual issuer disclosure behaviour.
Let Y denote potentially observable information, M the pattern of observed components, fθ(Y )
its model and gφ(M | Y ) the publication or missingness mechanism. The likelihood of the
realised data is
L(θ, φ) =
Z
fθ(yobs, ymis)gφ(m | yobs, ymis) dymis.
(19.1)
Here the realised pattern m fixes which coordinates are observed; the integration is over the
missing coordinates only. Discrete missing components use a sum instead. If, for the realised
observation, gφ(m | y) is constant over all compatible missing components, it factors out
of this integral. Together with distinct parameter spaces this yields the relevant likelihood
proportionality; a Bayesian factorisation also needs the appropriate prior independence. Seaman
and coauthors distinguish these realised-MAR conditions from the everywhere-MAR conditions
relevant to repeated-sampling comparisons (Seaman et al., 2013, Theorems 1–3, Section 5.4 and
Appendix). These are sufficient conditions for specified inferential equivalences. They do not
demonstrate that corporate missingness satisfies them.
19.19
When silence has an equilibrium interpretation
A separate local illustration makes non-disclosure endogenous. Let value V be uniform on
[0, 1]. A manager knows V with probability 1/2, independently of value; otherwise the manager
cannot disclose it. Informed disclosure is truthful and costless, the manager maximizes the
contemporaneous price, and risk-neutral investors price at conditional expected value. Suppose
an informed manager discloses above cutoff a. Silence occurs with probability (1 + a)/2. Its
unconditional value contribution is 1/4 + a2/4, giving silence price
Psilence(a) =
1 + a2
2(1 + a).
(19.2)
An informed manager at the cutoff is indifferent, so a = Psilence(a). Multiplying through and
collecting terms gives a2 + 2a −1 = 0, hence a =
√
2 −1 ≃0.4142. Types above that value
disclose and those below it prefer the silence price. The uninformed manager explains why
silence does not identify the worst type. This derivation is a local example; the blocked Dye
original is not claimed to have been reproduced.
Nor is favorable selection universal. Suijs models a firm that knows its return but is uncertain
how investors compare it with an outside opportunity (Suijs, 2007, Sections 3–4, Proposition 2
and Appendices A–B). Under the stated conditions a profitable firm can disclose lower returns
and withhold higher returns. Multiple partial-disclosure equilibria are possible. The implication
for AccDSL is to represent alternative disclosure mechanisms and test their consequences, rather
than hard-code one direction of selection.


PAGE 173
CHAPTER 19.
WHAT THE LITERATURES CONTRIBUTE
151
19.20
What changes under an intervention
A mechanical rate stress replaces an input while holding specified other relations fixed. A
conditional forecast computes outcomes among states where an observed rate has a chosen
value. A causal intervention changes the mechanism generating the rate and then propagates
the remaining structural relations. These calculations can disagree.
For a finite counterexample, let U be a fair binary variable and observe X = U, Y = U. An
alternative structural model has X = U, Y = X. Both give exactly the same observed joint
distribution. Under an intervention setting X = 1, the first model retains E[Y ] = 1/2 while the
second gives E[Y ] = 1. Observational agreement does not identify the intervention response. A
forecast labelled as causal therefore needs the structural assumptions that distinguish these
possibilities, or must retain the set of responses. Source corrections and simulated observations
also have different time meanings: a hypothetical future disclosure updates an actor inside that
run, not the analyst’s actual present evidence.
19.21
What remains to be researched
The survey is broad but not exhaustive. Important remaining questions include scenario-
reduction guarantees for adaptive company policies, robust filtering with static unidentified
parameters, strategic disclosure, multi-creditor restructuring, real-company policy estimation
and empirical forecast evaluation. Some require additional primary sources; others require
derivations and data specific to the target company.
The earlier Francis–Rowell simultaneous-equation lead remains technically uninspected because
attempted routes did not provide the paper. It cannot be declared inferior or irrelevant on that
basis. The missing Gentry derivation and source-version questions also remain explicit.
A source gap blocks the unsupported claim, not all independent work. The methods already
derived here can be specified and checked while the literature queue continues. This keeps
research honest without allowing an inaccessible paper to become a reason to stop every
implementation task.
19.22
The resulting method proposal
The synthesis favors a joint history family, dated contract and ownership state, actor-specific
information, explicit scenario semantics, feasible policy generation, event-to-accounting trans-
lation and query-driven computation. Each component has a distinct literature basis and a
distinct local obligation.
No single source validates their composition. The worked cases and proposed tests supply
initial evidence for the interfaces, while broader implementation and empirical studies remain
necessary. The design should therefore preserve the origin and scope of each assumption instead
of presenting the synthesis as an established off-the-shelf theory.
The next chapter turns this method proposal into concrete records and algorithms. Its engi-
neering choices remain replaceable. What must survive replacement is the meaning of evidence,
uncertainty, information, financing and result status developed throughout this part.


PAGE 174
Chapter 20
Implementing the proposed methods
without losing their meaning
The preceding chapters define what a result means. Implementation should preserve that
meaning across extraction, reconstruction, simulation and reporting. The engineering choices in
this chapter are proposals and can be replaced. The essential requirements are stable: evidence
is not an assumption, a history family is not a sample, actor information is not global state, a
financing request is not cash, and numerical failure is not default.
20.1
Evidence records
An evidence assertion needs a source identifier and exact location, value or text, concept,
entity, dimensions, units, basis, period and precision. It also needs publication and ingestion
information, extraction method and review status. A correction links to the assertion it revises
instead of deleting it.
The record should preserve original text alongside normalized values. A normalized number
can be checked against the source, and an unresolved phrase remains visible. A mapping from
source label to model concept is a separate interpretation with its own support. This allows the
mapping to change without pretending the source changed.
An evidence view selects assertions for a particular cutoff and purpose. It can represent as-
published, currently restated or analyst-reviewed information. The view should be immutable
once used for a frozen result. A new correction creates a new view and an explicit dependency
change.
20.2
Assumptions as first-class inputs
An assumption record identifies the quantity or relation supplied, its scope, provenance and role.
Examples include a fixed management payout policy, an offer price, a prior over debt types or a
terminal liquidation rule. A convenient default should be marked as a hypothesis or baseline
rather than disguised as observed evidence.
Assumptions can conflict. A policy allowing unlimited borrowing conflicts with an offer cap if
both claim to govern the same action. The system should detect or expose such inconsistencies.
Precedence rules must be explicit; silently favoring whichever input was loaded last is unsafe.
Sensitivity should vary assumptions by identity. If one assumption supplies both customer
152


PAGE 175
CHAPTER 20.
IMPLEMENTING THE PROPOSED METHODS
153
delay and credit loss, changing only one derived field can produce an inconsistent variant. A
structured assumption relation makes the intended intervention and its dependencies visible.
20.3
History families
A history family contains shared economic records, persistent unknowns, domains, constraints,
interpretation alternatives and source support. It records the domain for which completeness is
claimed and the method used to construct it. Optional witnesses are members, not substitutes
for the family.
Continuous variables should retain units and bounds. Discrete choices should retain mutually
exclusive premises where appropriate. Cross-time identity should connect a loan, customer or
asset across periods. A new contract version is a dated event, not a reassignment of an old
unknown.
The family can expose queries for feasibility, witnesses, possible answers, certain answers and
bounds. A probability operation requires an attached measure record. The API should not offer
an unconditional mean for an unweighted family without requesting or selecting an explicitly
declared measure.
20.4
Contract and security state
A contract record identifies parties, currency, dates, obligations, rights and clauses. Its execution
state records remaining principal, accrued amounts, past exercise and breach status. Accounting
carrying values are separate measurements linked to the contract.
A security register identifies classes, units, holders or categories, rights and settlement status.
It distinguishes authorized, subscribed, issued, outstanding, treasury and potentially issuable
units where the profile needs them. Conversion links the extinguished claim to created shares.
Unknown clauses should remain typed unknowns or alternatives. An unsupported clause should
block only claims depending on it, with an explicit reason. A simple loan profile can still
calculate unaffected payments while refusing a general default or option valuation. This is more
useful than either inventing a default clause or refusing every calculation.
20.5
Actor information
Each actor has an information specification: observable variables, private facts, report channels,
lags and memory. Policies receive a view constructed from that specification. They should
not receive the full simulated world and rely on programmers to remember which fields are
forbidden.
A policy invocation records its decision time and input view. Tests can then check that changing
unobserved future data leaves the action unchanged. A manager’s private contract knowledge
can be included without exposing it as actual public evidence.
Randomized policies need a declared randomization source independent of unavailable future
information. Reusing a shock draw as a policy coin can create unintended dependence. Economic
random variables should have stable identities and explicit coupling, not depend on incidental
call order.


PAGE 176
CHAPTER 20.
IMPLEMENTING THE PROPOSED METHODS
154
20.6
Scenario definitions
A scenario identifies external paths or distributions, interventions, horizon, event timing and
policy treatment. It distinguishes a deterministic stress from observational conditioning and a
causal intervention. A scenario can also define alternative future observation processes.
Shock units should be typed: a two-percentage-point rate increase differs from a two per cent
proportional increase. Currency rates need orientation. Price levels differ from returns. The
scenario layer should reject ambiguous conversions rather than rely on labels.
A scenario version should include the underlying risk-process and observation versions. Changing
a future disclosure rule can change policy values even when the rate path is unchanged. The
cache and provenance system must recognize that dependency.
20.7
Event proposals and admission
A policy proposes an action. The event service checks contractual rights, resource availability,
counterparty acceptance and timing. An accepted action creates dated events; a rejected action
returns a reason and leaves the state unchanged except for any explicitly modeled attempt cost.
Financing proposals should include requested cash or quantity, eligible offers and settlement
conditions. The service calculates proceeds, fees, promises and ownership consequences. It does
not infer unlimited supply from a balance identity.
Admission can be conditional over a history region. If an unknown covenant makes an offer
feasible in some histories and infeasible in others, the service returns guarded outcomes. It
should not choose one witness merely to avoid branching.
20.8
Ordering events
The scheduler orders economic events by effective time and declared precedence. Same-time
events can still have dependencies: proceeds must arrive before they fund payment, and a reset
can occur before or after accrual according to contract terms. A total ordering chosen arbitrarily
by event identifier can change results.
A partial-order representation can express independent events and necessary precedences. If
simultaneous equations are genuinely required, they should be passed to a solver rather than
forced into a sequential order. The result should distinguish an unresolved ordering from
numerical nonconvergence.
Time conversion needs explicit precision and timezone rules. A local midnight, 23:59:59 and
next-day midnight are not interchangeable in a boundary-sensitive query. The existing exact
timing work in the repository is a foundation; external contract adapters must preserve its
distinctions or state their loss of precision.
20.9
Accounting rule application
After an admitted economic event, the accounting service selects a rule under the declared
basis, jurisdiction, entity facts and effective period. It produces a complete posting bundle and
supporting measurements. Missing authority or facts can yield conditional treatments or a
typed refusal.


PAGE 177
CHAPTER 20.
IMPLEMENTING THE PROPOSED METHODS
155
Posting bundles are validated atomically. They reconcile debits and credits, currencies, account
contexts and event-specific quantities.
An event identifier prevents duplicate application.
Reversal and correction events refer to the original bundle rather than editing it invisibly.
The reporting service aggregates within a specified perimeter and period. It preserves source-to-
event-to-journal-to-line provenance. A consolidated report can eliminate internal balances while
retaining the underlying entity records for liquidity and legal-entity analysis.
20.10
Symbolic execution
A symbolic state contains expressions and constraints rather than one numeric company. An
event whose rule is common across the region can update expressions directly. A guard partitions
the region only when its truth changes the relevant event or output.
The executor should preserve coverage of the parent region and distinguish disjoint alternatives
from overlapping descriptions. Overlap can duplicate probability mass if weights are attached.
A symbolic union should therefore use semantic region identity rather than merely concatenating
records.
Simplification can share subexpressions and eliminate variables under checked projection rules.
Any approximation records an outer or inner relation, error target and scope. A witness
extracted from an outer approximation must be validated before being presented as a possible
history.
20.11
Optional probability measures
A measure record identifies support, weights or densities, parameterization, conditioning variables
and source assumptions. It is attached to economic variables and histories, not to the number
of stored nodes. Duplicate encodings should not change the measure.
Updates record likelihood and evidence identity. Reusing the same evidence twice should
be detected where the model treats it as one observation. Correlated sources require a joint
observation model or an explicit approximation.
Simulation can carry both the retained feasible family and an approximate measure. A particle
filter’s support loss does not delete the original family. A user can request probability estimates
