under one measure and robust bounds over the family without conflating them.
20.12
Query planning
A query names outputs, dates, actors, scenarios, policies and acceptable guarantees. The
planner traces dependencies through contracts, guards, observations and reports. It selects exact
symbolic calculation, optimization, enumeration or approximation according to the admitted
profile.
The planner should prefer the smallest method that answers the question. An affine interest
range does not require a million paths. A policy-sensitive signal problem cannot be answered by
a fixed-path cash table. A worst-case query cannot be answered by an unlabeled Monte Carlo
minimum.
Resource budgets are explicit. If the selected exact method cannot finish, the planner may
return bounds or ask for an allowed approximation according to the request. It should not


PAGE 178
CHAPTER 20.
IMPLEMENTING THE PROPOSED METHODS
156
silently downgrade a guarantee. Useful partial results include verified witnesses and certified
safe regions with unresolved remainder.
20.13
Result records
A result identifies its target and scope, evidence and assumptions, conditional regions, values
or formulas, measure if any, numerical status and provenance. It can contain exact values,
intervals, distributions, witnesses and unresolved statuses together. Their semantics should be
explicit.
Possible failure, certain failure and unresolved failure are different answers. A breach is different
from default. An infeasible proposed action differs from a company with no feasible action in the
modeled class. An unsupported mechanism differs from numerical failure. These distinctions
should survive both machine output and reader-facing explanation.
Aggregates must retain unresolved regions or mass. If some paths fail numerically, reporting
only successful paths can bias results. The system should state what portion of the admitted
family or probability remains unevaluated and avoid renormalization unless that conditional
target is explicitly requested.
20.14
A small request sequence
A user asks whether cash can fall below 14 after a rate and customer shock. The system selects
an evidence view and reconstructs the four-history family of Chapter 13. It applies the stated
events, obtains cash formula 33 −18c −2f, and solves the threshold condition.
The result returns the concentrated floating witness with cash 13 and the other three outcomes.
It states that no probabilities are attached. If the user adds a committed facility, the event
service checks its timing and eligibility, creates a draw in the required region and recomputes
debt and cash.
If a new source excludes the concentrated floating combination, the family version changes and
the query’s dependency triggers recomputation. The old result remains reproducible. This
sequence demonstrates the intended workflow without requiring every possible company to be
instantiated as a separate database.
20.15
DSL and typed-library authoring
A textual language can make scenario and contract definitions readable and reviewable. A typed
library can offer direct programmatic construction. Both should lower to the same intermediate
representation and be compared on the same tasks. The DSL is not justified merely by its
existence.
Equivalent authored inputs should produce equivalent IR and results. Syntax convenience should
not alter uncertainty or timing. A textual default that silently assumes a rate or probability
would be especially dangerous because it can look like a declarative fact.
The existing repository includes language, expression, method and event work that should
be reused where it matches these semantics. New records should be added only when an
included operation cannot be expressed adequately. The design should avoid rebuilding a
parallel incompatible representation for the new chapters.


PAGE 179
CHAPTER 20.
IMPLEMENTING THE PROPOSED METHODS
157
20.16
Adapters to external components
An external contract engine can propose schedules and payoffs. An optimization package can
solve a declared mathematical problem. A probabilistic library can approximate a posterior.
Each adapter needs input and output semantics, version identity, numerical conventions and
failure mapping.
The ACTUS inspection illustrates the boundary: contract events do not supply accounting
journals, and local-time schedules do not automatically preserve exact timestamps. A double-
valued payoff is not exact rational evidence merely because its printed decimal matches a
fixture.
Adapters should be checked against small independent cases and preserve unsupported outcomes.
A service exception must not become zero cash flow. A missing risk series must not silently
carry forward an unrelated value. Dependency and license review remain necessary before
selecting a runtime, but no external adoption is claimed by this manuscript.
20.17
Incremental implementation
The first vertical slice should support a bounded history family, exact event timing, simple debt
and equity offers, actor information and coherent statements. It should include both successful
and refused paths. The worked company and refinancing cases provide expected outcomes.
The next slice can add class rights, conversion, group transfers and persistent tax or asset state.
Later work can add nonlinear pricing, partially observed policy optimization and controlled
approximation. Each stage should preserve the full mission’s open requirements rather than
imply that a small slice closes whole-company representation.
A slice is complete only when its source-to-result behavior is checked and its unsupported scope
is explicit. A collection of isolated module tests is useful but insufficient for composition. The
critical interactions are where financing, information and accounting meet.
20.18
Review and release evidence
A release should preserve model and source versions, exact fixture results, numerical validation
where applicable and rendered documentation. Engineering conformance, accounting authority,
scientific validity and human comprehension remain separate evidence categories.
A clean PDF or passing arithmetic script does not establish product readiness. A reader-facing
explanation must still make assumptions and derivations understandable. A human review can
identify comprehension problems that mechanical checks miss; its absence does not prevent
provisional drafting but does prevent claiming that acceptance occurred.
The stable documentation links should point to a built, identified candidate with an honest status.
Historical manuscripts and hashes remain available. This allows the design to advance without
rewriting evidence from earlier stages or confusing the newest document with a completed
system.


PAGE 180
Chapter 21
Groups, counterparties, and
contagion across otherwise coherent
statements
A company group is not a single bank account. Consolidation can eliminate internal balances
for reporting while cash remains held by particular entities, currencies and custodians. Stress
can also propagate through guarantees, collateral and common counterparties. These mecha-
nisms make group simulation a natural test of whether the model preserves economic rights
beyond accounting totals. The examples here stipulate transfer and priority rules; actual legal
applicability requires source-specific authority.
21.1
Consolidated cash and entity cash
Consider a parent P and subsidiary S. P holds cash 5 and owes external debt 20 tomorrow. S
holds cash 30 and owes external suppliers 8 the following day. The consolidated cash balance is
35. It exceeds the parent’s debt maturity, but that fact does not establish that P can pay.
Suppose S may transfer at most 10 before tomorrow because the remainder is restricted or
needed under a binding local rule. P can assemble 15 and has a shortfall of 5. A consolidated
cash forecast that deducts 20 from 35 reports 15 remaining and misses the entity failure.
If a transfer of 15 is permitted instead, P can pay but S’s remaining cash must still cover its
own obligations. The transfer is not free group liquidity: it changes where resources are held
and can create an intercompany claim or distribution. The event should be represented in both
entities before consolidation.
21.2
Intercompany loan mechanics
Suppose S lends 15 to P. S records an intercompany receivable and cash outflow; P records cash
and an intercompany payable. P then repays external debt 20 using its original 5 plus the loan.
Consolidated cash falls from 35 to 15 and external debt falls by 20. Internal claims eliminate in
the consolidated statement under the stipulated perimeter.
The internal loan nevertheless matters for future entity liquidity. P owes S according to its terms.
If S later needs cash, it cannot spend a consolidated elimination entry. A model that deletes
internal contracts after consolidation loses repayment dates and possible stress transmission.
158


PAGE 181
CHAPTER 21.
GROUPS, COUNTERPARTIES, AND CONTAGION
159
Interest on the internal loan can affect entity tax and distributable earnings even when consoli-
dated interest eliminates. Currency differences can create further effects. The general engine
should retain legal-entity records and produce consolidation as a reporting view rather than
using the consolidated view as its only state.
21.3
A guarantee creates contingent support
Suppose P guarantees S’s external supplier obligation of 8. The guarantee produces no cash
payment while S pays normally. If S fails, P may be required to pay under the stipulated terms.
The group’s reported balance sheet may not reveal the same exposure as a direct loan.
A scenario that stresses S can therefore affect P even when there is no planned intercompany
transfer. The guarantee needs beneficiary, covered obligation, trigger, cap, expiry and settlement
timing. A vague statement of support is not automatically an enforceable guarantee. Conversely,
omitting a binding guarantee because it has no current principal balance understates contingent
funding need.
If P’s payment gives it a recovery claim against S, that successor claim should be recorded.
Paying the guarantee and also leaving the supplier fully paid by S would double settle the
obligation unless the model includes reimbursement or another event. Counterparty identities
make this check possible.
21.4
A common collateral pool
Two loans may claim the same collateral.
A model that assigns the full collateral value
independently to each overstates recovery. Let collateral realize 50, with secured claims 40 and
30. Under an assumed first-ranking claim of 40 and second-ranking claim of 30, recoveries are
40 and 10.
If the claims rank equally and share proportionally, recoveries are 50(40/70) and 50(30/70).
Both allocations conserve 50 but differ by creditor. Priority is an economic and legal fact, not
an accounting balancing choice.
The collateral record should link each asset to encumbrances and ranking. Unknown ranking
creates alternative recovery histories. An aggregate secured-debt amount does not identify
whether collateral is shared, separate or overpledged. A stress result should preserve that
ambiguity when it affects pricing or liquidity.
21.5
Collateral value and borrowing capacity
A borrowing-base facility can limit drawn debt to a fraction of eligible collateral. Suppose
eligible receivables are 100 and the advance rate is 0.8, giving capacity 80. Drawn debt is
70. A customer default removes 20 from eligibility, reducing capacity to 64 and creating a
borrowing-base deficiency of 6.
The credit loss has two effects: it reduces asset value and can trigger a cash repayment or
collateral substitution requirement. A model that records only the impairment misses the
liquidity consequence. A model that records a repayment without the contractual deficiency
rule invents one.
Eligibility can depend on age, concentration, jurisdiction and disputes. Two receivable pools
with equal net carrying value can have different borrowing capacity. The reconstruction may


PAGE 182
CHAPTER 21.
GROUPS, COUNTERPARTIES, AND CONTAGION
160
need invoice cohorts or symbolic eligibility amounts even when the financial statement reports
one total.
21.6
A margin call before cash income
A derivative can economically hedge a future exposure while requiring collateral today. Suppose
a company has a fixed-rate asset whose economic value rises when rates fall and a derivative
position that offsets that value movement but requires cash margin after an adverse mark. The
ultimate hedge can reduce value risk while increasing short-term liquidity need.
The cash call depends on collateral agreement, thresholds, minimum transfer amounts, eligible
assets and settlement timing. It cannot be inferred from the derivative’s year-end fair value
alone. A scenario that nets the derivative and underlying value change may correctly describe
net economic exposure while missing gross cash settlement.
The simulator should track valuation, collateral posting and final payoff as distinct events.
Posted collateral may remain an asset or claim under the applicable treatment; it is not
necessarily an expense. Reusing the fair-value change as a cash flow can create double counting.
21.7
Currency trapped in the wrong account
Suppose P owes 10 foreign units and holds 100 domestic units. At an executable exchange
rate of 8 domestic per foreign, conversion needs 80. Under a depreciation to 12, it needs
120.
If conversion capacity is limited or markets close before payment, even a favorable
reporting-currency total may not be usable.
Now suppose S holds 10 foreign units but cannot transfer them to P in time. Consolidated
foreign cash equals the payment, yet P still faces a shortfall. A model should retain both
currency and entity location. Netting by currency across the group can be as misleading as
netting all currencies into one reporting amount.
A permitted currency swap or intercompany transfer can solve the problem, but it introduces
counterparties, settlement risk and possible costs. The model should generate those transactions
rather than assume frictionless conversion as an unnamed default.
21.8
Settlement risk
Two offsetting payments on the same date need not settle simultaneously. A company may pay
one currency before receiving another. A counterparty failure between the legs can leave an
exposure. A net daily cash amount does not describe the gross settlement sequence.
For a bounded example, P pays 80 domestic units at noon and expects 10 foreign units at 15:00.
Its foreign debt is due at 14:00. Even if both legs settle as promised, the receipt is too late. A
settlement service with payment-versus-payment or a bridge can change the sequence, but must
be represented.
Exact intraday simulation is unnecessary for every query. It becomes necessary when ordering
affects payment feasibility. The method should allow event resolution to increase around material
boundaries without pretending that an annual model resolved them.


PAGE 183
CHAPTER 21.
GROUPS, COUNTERPARTIES, AND CONTAGION
161
21.9
Cross-default and acceleration
A default on one obligation can trigger rights under another. The triggering event may be
nonpayment, acceleration, insolvency or a threshold amount. A generic flag called default
cannot substitute for the actual cross-default clause.
Suppose S misses 8 and P has a loan of 50 whose clause triggers only for subsidiary defaults
exceeding 10. The event does not meet that threshold. If the clause instead aggregates several
defaults totaling 12, it may trigger. The scope, aggregation period and cure rights matter.
Acceleration changes the payment calendar and can create a liquidity spiral. The model should
record the trigger, exercise or automatic effect, new due date and notice requirements under
the stipulated contract. It should not accelerate every group obligation whenever any branch is
distressed.
21.10
A contagion sequence
Combine the borrowing-base example with a parent guarantee. A customer default reduces S’s
eligible receivables from 100 to 80. The facility demands repayment 6. S has cash 4 and cannot
pay the full amount. Under the example’s guarantee, P pays the remaining 2. P’s cash falls
from 5 to 3.
If P has an independent payment of 4 later that day, the support creates a parent shortfall of 1.
Consolidated asset loss alone does not show the sequence. The guarantee transfers liquidity
pressure across entities.
If the guarantee is capped at 1, S remains in arrears by 1 and P retains cash 4. The parent can
meet its payment, but subsidiary distress persists. Different guarantee terms produce different
locations and timing of default even when consolidated net assets are similar.
21.11
Behavioral contagion
Counterparties may react to distress before a contractual trigger. Suppliers can shorten terms,
customers can delay orders, lenders can decline new credit and employees can leave. These
responses are behavioral assumptions or estimated mechanisms. They should not be inferred
automatically from a balance-sheet loss.
A scenario can include a supplier-term tightening rule conditional on a public rating event. The
rule needs the event’s timing and actor information. If suppliers have not observed the distress,
immediate tightening may give them unavailable knowledge. If they have private signals, those
should be specified.
Behavioral contagion can amplify losses, but an arbitrary amplification coefficient is not evidence.
The model should expose sensitivity and distinguish contractual consequences from discretionary
reactions. This separation makes the result more useful for identifying which mechanisms require
empirical research.
21.12
Endogenous prices and fire sales
Selling assets to meet cash needs can depress their prices when the market is illiquid or several
firms sell simultaneously. A fixed-price sale model may then overstate proceeds. An endogenous
price rule creates feedback between liquidity need, quantity sold and realized value.


PAGE 184
CHAPTER 21.
GROUPS, COUNTERPARTIES, AND CONTAGION
162
For illustration, let sale price per unit be p(q) = 10 −0.1q for 0 ≤q ≤50. Proceeds are
10q −0.1q2, which increase on this interval but at a decreasing rate. Raising 200 requires solving
the quadratic rather than dividing by the initial price 10.
The formula is a teaching assumption. It should not be treated as an estimated market impact
curve. Its role is to expose how quantity and price can interact. Beyond the stated domain,
the rule may be nonsensical. A solver should not extrapolate it to negative prices to force a
solution.
21.13
Feedback across several sellers
If price depends on total sales by several entities, each policy affects the others’ proceeds.
Sequentially solving each entity at an unchanged price can be inconsistent. A simultaneous
market-clearing or declared sequential mechanism is needed.
The agent-based stock-flow literature provides examples of interacting counterparties and explicit
event order (Caiani et al., 2016). It does not imply that a specific market-impact function is
valid for the company under study. The local mechanism must be stated and tested.
Multiple equilibria or no feasible clearing outcome can arise.
A numerical failure to find
one should remain distinct from a proved absence. If the model selects one equilibrium by
a behavioral adjustment rule, that rule is part of the scenario and should be retained in
provenance.
21.14
Consolidation changes after distress
A restructuring can change control, ownership or the reporting perimeter. A subsidiary may be
sold, placed into a process that changes control, or receive new capital. The group statements
then require a perimeter transition under the applicable basis.
The economic event should identify assets, liabilities, consideration and retained rights. The
accounting layer determines derecognition, gains, losses and continuing interests. A generic
default event that removes the entire subsidiary from consolidation without an authority-based
control assessment is unsupported.
The scenario can remain useful with conditional perimeter outcomes if facts are unresolved.
The result should show which cash and obligations remain in each legal entity and how each
accounting interpretation reports them. A single consolidated number can conceal materially
different legal exposures.
21.15
A group-state proposal
The state should include legal entities, ownership and control assessments, currency accounts,
external and internal contracts, collateral pools, guarantees and transfer permissions. It should
also retain actor-specific information and event calendars. Consolidated reporting is a derived
view.
This does not require every query to load every field. Dependency-aware execution can select
the relevant subgraph. A parent liquidity query follows transfers and guarantees; a consolidated
revenue query follows perimeter and eliminations. The underlying representation preserves the
links needed when the question changes.


PAGE 185
CHAPTER 21.
GROUPS, COUNTERPARTIES, AND CONTAGION
163
Unknown links can be represented as alternatives. A guarantee’s existence, cap or trigger may
be unresolved. The query can report outcomes conditional on those terms and identify the
source that would resolve them. Inventing no guarantee as a default would systematically favor
benign outcomes.
21.16
Tests for group coherence
A transfer test should show equal and opposite cash movements before fees and consistent
internal claims. A consolidation test should eliminate internal balances without deleting entity
state. A collateral test should prevent recovery exceeding the shared pool. A timing test should
prevent late cash from funding an earlier obligation.
A guarantee test should create the correct successor claim or loss under the stipulated rules
and avoid double payment. A currency test should preserve native units through conversion
and reporting. A cross-default test should distinguish threshold, cure and acceleration.
These tests are stronger than a final consolidated balance-sheet equality. They examine the
mechanisms that can make a group fail despite apparently sufficient aggregate resources. Passing
them establishes the specified small cases, while actual legal and regulatory applicability remains
tied to source evidence.
21.17
What cannot be compressed away
For group liquidity, entity location, currency, transfer rights and dates are often essential.
For recovery, collateral and priority are essential. For behavior, information and contractual
discretion are essential. A compression that removes them may still reproduce current totals
while changing stress outcomes.
The practical method is to retain a rich underlying state and derive query-specific summaries.
A summary approved for one purpose can be refined for another. This avoids both extremes:
duplicating the entire group for every possibility and collapsing the group into totals that cannot
explain who can pay whom.


PAGE 186
Chapter 22
When the model itself is incomplete:
missingness, misspecification, and
research priorities
Preserving all histories inside a model does not protect against a missing mechanism outside it.
A feasible family can be exact and still omit a guarantee, a customer concentration or a financing
restriction. This chapter examines how to represent that limitation without abandoning useful
analysis. The aim is to make conditional conclusions precise and to direct further work toward
omissions that can change decisions.
22.1
Unknown value versus absent mechanism
An unknown interest rate can be represented by a variable if the contract form is known. An
unknown existence of a guarantee requires an alternative contract structure. A mechanism the
language cannot express is a different limitation. These cases should not share one generic null.
A missing value within a supported form can often be bounded or inferred. A missing structural
alternative can change the dimension and event logic of the family. An unsupported mechanism
may require a new model component before any bound is meaningful. The result should identify
which kind of gap it faces.
For example, allowing a loan rate between zero and twenty per cent does not cover a missed
conversion right. A wider rate interval cannot reproduce the ownership change caused by
conversion. Numerical sensitivity over existing parameters is not a substitute for structural
coverage.
22.2
The disclosure package defines an evidence boundary
A company filing can incorporate other documents by reference or discuss risks without
quantifying them. A reconstruction based only on primary statements may omit material terms
in notes, exhibits or subsequent events. The evidence package should state which documents
and passages were considered.
Coverage has stages: retrieval, parsing, interpretation and model admission. A page can be
retrieved but not parsed; parsed but not understood; understood but outside the current
executable profile. These distinctions matter for the completeness claim.
164


PAGE 187
CHAPTER 22.
WHEN THE MODEL ITSELF IS INCOMPLETE
165
An unresolved passage should remain linked to relevant model areas. A guarantee discussion
may block a claim that all contingent obligations are represented. It need not block a simple
historical revenue reconciliation. Scope should be query-sensitive rather than all-or-nothing.
22.3
Missingness can be informative
A missing disclosure may result from immateriality, reporting exemptions, extraction failure or
deliberate omission within a strategic disclosure model. These causes have different implications.
Treating every missing value as zero assumes the most benign explanation. Treating every
missing value as arbitrarily large can make analysis useless.
The first task is to identify the source of missingness where possible. If a table was not extracted
from a scanned page, the remedy is data processing. If the filing does not disclose a contract
term, the uncertainty is substantive. If a disclosure is legally required under certain facts,
inference from absence requires actual authority and evidence about compliance; it cannot be
assumed.
A probabilistic model of missingness can be added when justified. It should state whether
missingness depends on hidden exposure or actor behavior. An assumption of missing at
random is not a universal default for corporate disclosure. Without support, retain alternative
explanations rather than fabricate a likelihood.
22.4
Conflicting sources
An annual report, interim filing and debt exhibit can disagree. The disagreement can reflect
different dates, scopes, currencies, amendments or errors. A model that takes the latest number
without reconciling context can discard valid historical information.
Source precedence should depend on the claim. A later amendment may supersede future
contract terms while leaving past payments unchanged. A restated financial statement may
revise a comparative amount. A management presentation may use a nonstandard metric rather
than contradict a filing.
The evidence view should preserve both assertions and the interpretation linking them. If
conflict remains unresolved, the family may contain alternative source-admission views or return
an explicit contradiction. It should not average incompatible facts merely because both are
numeric.
22.5
Measurement error and reporting discretion
A discrepancy between predicted and reported amounts can arise from rounding, estimation,
extraction error or an incorrect economic history. A single Gaussian residual model collapses
these mechanisms. It may be convenient statistically but can imply implausible support and
independence.
Rounding often gives bounded intervals. Extraction errors can be discrete shifts in sign or
scale. Accounting estimates can have structured dependence on assumptions and information.
Reporting discretion may be strategic. The observation model should reflect the mechanism
relevant to the claim.
A robust likelihood or contamination model can be considered, but its parameters need prove-
nance and sensitivity analysis.
It should not become a way to make every contradiction


PAGE 188
CHAPTER 22.
WHEN THE MODEL ITSELF IS INCOMPLETE
166
disappear. A large residual may be evidence that the model omitted a transaction rather than
merely noisy reporting.
22.6
Structural alternatives
Suppose revenue is modeled as price times quantity. A different model includes subscription
renewals, usage charges and cancellation options. Both may fit historical totals. A demand
shock can produce different future responses because the contract structures differ.
The family can include alternative structural models with shared observations and explicit
scope. Comparing their conditional outcomes identifies model risk. Assigning posterior weights
requires model priors and likelihoods; otherwise the alternatives remain unweighted.
A structural alternative should be concrete enough to simulate or bound. A vague statement that
anything could happen is not useful. The modeler should identify plausible omitted mechanisms,
their source support and the smallest discriminating evidence. This makes uncertainty actionable
without claiming completeness.
22.7
Behavioral parameter uncertainty
A collection-delay parameter estimated from historical data can be uncertain and unstable.
A recession can change customer behavior, credit terms or the company’s enforcement policy.
Treating the estimate as a fixed fact understates uncertainty.
Separate estimation uncertainty within a behavioral form from uncertainty about the form
itself. A confidence interval around a constant collection rate does not cover a regime switch
in which major customers stop paying. Conversely, a broad regime set may be unnecessarily
pessimistic for a short, well-understood contract schedule.
The scenario should state whether behavior is held fixed, estimated conditionally, changed by
a stress rule or optimized. Each treatment answers a different question. An empirical policy
estimated under one financing regime may not transfer after covenants bind.
22.8
Extrapolation
A model calibrated in low-rate periods can be applied to high-rate stress. Contractual cash
formulas may remain valid if terms are known, while behavioral relationships may not. The
extrapolation status should be component-specific.
A floating coupon formula with a known cap can be evaluated outside historical rates because
the contract supplies the rule. A regression of sales on rates may have no support in that region.
A neural surrogate trained only below the cap may also fail even though the underlying contract
formula is simple.
The model should distinguish extrapolation of an economic law, a behavioral estimate and a
numerical approximation. Each requires different evidence. A global warning that the scenario
is extreme is less useful than identifying which components are unsupported and how that
affects the result.


PAGE 189
CHAPTER 22.
WHEN THE MODEL ITSELF IS INCOMPLETE
167
22.9
Unknown unknowns and explicit coverage
A model cannot enumerate mechanisms it has not conceived. It can, however, maintain a
coverage map organized by assets, obligations, rights, entities, timing and information. Review
can ask whether material disclosures have a place in that map.
Stress testing the representation itself is useful. Introduce an unexpected guarantee, a delayed
tax payment or a conversion clause and see whether the system can preserve it as unresolved
rather than dropping it. A robust data model should fail visibly when a new mechanism is
outside its profile.
This is different from claiming that a checklist proves completeness. The coverage map supports
systematic discovery. It remains extensible as new cases arise. The manuscript’s failure catalogue
should be read in that spirit.
22.10
Sensitivity to the family boundary
Suppose a result is safe for every admitted history. The conclusion can still depend on the
family’s bounds. If floating share is limited to 0.4 by a weak assumption rather than a source,
expanding it to one can change safety.
A useful analysis identifies active assumptions: those whose relaxation changes the conclusion or
its bound. This can be done through alternative runs, dual information in suitable optimization
problems or counterexample search. The method should not infer economic importance solely
from a numerical coefficient.
An assumption may be inactive for one query and decisive for another. A maturity bound
irrelevant to next month’s interest can dominate a one-year refinancing stress. Sensitivity
records should therefore be tied to query and horizon.
22.11
Partial identification of a decision
The evidence may not identify every parameter but still identify the preferred action. If one
policy dominates another across all compatible histories, further historical detail is unnecessary
for that comparison. Conversely, a narrow parameter interval can still straddle a decision
threshold.
This distinction directs research effort. The goal is not always to recover the true journal
uniquely. It may be enough to establish a robust decision or a bounded exposure. A history
family is useful precisely because it can show which unresolved details matter.
The system should therefore report both parameter uncertainty and decision sensitivity. A
large uncertainty set need not imply indecision, and a small set need not imply safety. The
geometry relative to policies and thresholds is what matters.
22.12
Research priorities from counterexamples
A verified adverse witness can reveal the missing fact that drives risk. If the witness requires
a near-term foreign-currency maturity, the next source target is a currency-maturity cross-
classification. If it requires a guarantee, inspect the guarantee terms. If it requires refinancing
refusal, investigate committed capacity and draw conditions.


PAGE 190
CHAPTER 22.
WHEN THE MODEL ITSELF IS INCOMPLETE
168
The witness should be accompanied by competing interpretations and checked consequences.
This is useful internal preparation before asking a professional a specific question. An unresolved
label alone is not a review-ready brief.
The repository’s internal-preparation requirement therefore fits the method: finish feasible
source retrieval, calculations and competing interpretations first. No professional engagement
or expenditure is authorized by the existence of an open accounting or legal question. Ordinary
reader feedback is a separate need.
22.13
Model revision without losing history
When a missing mechanism is added, old results may change. Preserve the old model and result
with their scope, then create a revised version. This allows users to distinguish a forecast error
under the old model from a correction to the target definition.
A new guarantee can expand the family or add obligations. A new disclosure can narrow it. A
corrected accounting mapping can change reported outcomes without changing economic cash.
These revisions have different meanings and should not all be called data refreshes.
Dependency tracking should identify which results need recomputation. A source affecting
one loan should not require rebuilding unrelated evidence, but its effects may propagate
through liquidity, default and group guarantees. The dependency graph must follow economic
consequences as well as direct formulas.
22.14
Communicating model risk
A useful explanation states the result, the assumptions that drive it and the specific unresolved
mechanisms that could change it. It does not bury the conclusion under generic warnings. For
example, “cash remains above 14 for all retained histories provided the facility remains drawable
after customer default” identifies a meaningful condition.
If that condition is not established, say so directly and show the alternative result. Avoid
presenting a conditional safe outcome as unconditional safety. Equally, do not describe every
unresolved mechanism as inevitable disaster. The family should distinguish possible, necessary
and unassessed consequences.
The reader should be able to see whether more evidence, a different policy or a better computation
would resolve the issue. These are different next steps. A numerical approximation gap cannot
be closed by another disclosure if the model already knows the relevant fact; a missing contract
clause cannot be closed by more solver iterations.
22.15
A defensible stopping point
A query can be complete within a declared scope even when the entire company model is
incomplete. Completion means that the requested target has been computed with the stated
guarantees and that material excluded mechanisms are disclosed. It does not mean all uncertainty
has disappeared.
A broader product-readiness claim requires broader coverage and evidence. The 150–300-page
treatment is a documentation deliverable, not proof of that readiness. The manuscript remains a
candidate whose assumptions, sources and proposed methods require continued implementation
and human review.


PAGE 191
CHAPTER 22.
WHEN THE MODEL ITSELF IS INCOMPLETE
169
This distinction permits useful progress. It avoids both false certainty and paralysis. The
system can answer bounded questions accurately, retain uncertainty honestly and identify the
concrete work needed to support stronger conclusions.


PAGE 192
Chapter 23
Memory across reporting periods:
taxes, vintages, arrears, and future
rights
Two companies with the same current statements can differ because their past creates different
future rights and obligations. A simulation that keeps only current totals forgets this memory.
This chapter follows several small examples over more than one period. The examples use
stipulated accounting and tax rules solely to expose state requirements. They are not claims
about a particular jurisdiction’s current law.
23.1
A loss carryforward
Assume a teaching tax system with rate 25 per cent, no refund for current losses, unlimited
use of carried losses against future positive taxable income and no expiry. Let Lt be unused
losses entering period t and Tt taxable income before using them. For positive Tt, used losses
are Ut = min(Lt, Tt). Cash tax and next loss balance are
Taxt = 0.25 max(Tt −Lt, 0),
(23.1)
Lt+1 = max(Lt −max(Tt, 0), 0) + max(−Tt, 0).
(23.2)
The second equation covers both profit and loss periods under these simplified rules. A positive
income reduces available losses; a negative income adds its magnitude.
Company A enters with losses 40 and company B with none. Both have the same current cash,
assets and book equity because the example assumes no recognized deferred tax asset. Next
period taxable income is 30. A pays no cash tax and retains losses 10. B pays 7.5. Current
statements alone do not identify that difference.
In the following period taxable income is again 30. A uses its remaining 10 and pays 5; B pays
7.5. Over two periods, tax is 5 versus 15. Omitting loss memory overstates A’s tax. Treating
the original loss as an immediate refund would instead overstate its initial cash. Both errors
can occur in simple effective-tax-rate forecasts.
23.2
Limits and expiry change the recurrence
Now impose a rule that losses can offset at most 50 per cent of positive taxable income in a
period. With L0 = 40 and T0 = 30, usable losses are 15, tax is 3.75 and remaining losses are 25.
170


PAGE 193
CHAPTER 23.
MEMORY ACROSS SEVERAL REPORTING PERIODS
171
The earlier recurrence is no longer correct.
An expiry date requires vintage-specific loss balances. A single total 40 cannot determine which
amount expires next year. Two companies with the same total can have different future tax
capacity. The state may need a vector by expiry cohort, jurisdiction and restriction.
A change of ownership can affect utilization under some actual systems, but no such rule is
assumed here without authority. The point is structural: tax rights depend on facts beyond a
total balance. The model should admit the applicable rule and preserve the state it requires.
23.3
Deferred tax is a measurement, not the loss pool
A deferred tax asset may be recognized only under specified accounting criteria. Its carrying
amount can differ from the mechanical tax value of losses. A valuation allowance or recognition
restriction can change book equity without changing the legal loss pool.
Suppose the legal losses remain 40 while a recognized deferred tax asset is reduced from 10
to 4 under the chosen assessment. The reduction is not a cash tax payment. The future tax
computation still needs the legal loss balance and applicable utilization rules.
Conversely, recognizing an asset of 10 does not create 10 of spendable cash. A liquidity scenario
should use actual tax payment or refund dates. The accounting measurement and economic tax
rights are linked but distinct states.
23.4
Asset vintages with the same carrying amount
Consider two machines under a simple straight-line model with zero residual value. Machine A
originally cost 100 and has two of ten years remaining, so its carrying amount is 20 and annual
depreciation 10. Machine B originally cost 40 and has one of two years remaining, also carrying
20 but annual depreciation 20. Equal current carrying amount does not imply equal next-year
expense.
If the reported current depreciation is also constrained to match, combinations of several
machines can still create ambiguity. For example, a portfolio can mix assets with different
remaining lives whose total charge is the same. The next-period charge changes when a cohort
reaches the end of life.
The state should retain acquisition or remaining-life cohorts where depreciation, maintenance
or replacement depends on them. A single average life may be adequate for a smooth aggregate
forecast but can miss a replacement wall. The approximation should be checked against the
horizon and decision.
23.5
Replacement and operating capacity
An asset can be fully depreciated while still productive, or retain book value while becoming
unusable. Depreciation is not a direct physical failure process. Replacing capital expenditure
with depreciation assumes a relationship that must be justified separately.
Suppose A’s machine can operate for two more years without replacement and B’s fails after
one year unless 30 is spent. Both enter with carrying amount 20. A financing shock in year
two affects B’s production but not A’s in the same way. A model using only carrying amount
cannot distinguish them.


PAGE 194
CHAPTER 23.
MEMORY ACROSS SEVERAL REPORTING PERIODS
172
A richer state includes capacity, maintenance, failure or retirement rule and replacement lead
time. These can be uncertain. The history family may retain alternative vintage and condition
profiles compatible with the same accounting data. Their scenario outcomes remain conditional
rather than being collapsed into one average machine.
23.6
Arrears are not current-period expense again
Suppose a loan coupon of 5 accrues in period one but is not paid. Under the illustrative accrual
treatment, interest expense and a payable increase by 5. In period two, paying the arrears
reduces cash and the payable. It does not recognize the same interest expense again.
If penalty interest accrues on arrears, that is an additional contractual event with its own
base, rate and dates. The state must retain unpaid amounts and payment allocation rules. A
payment may be applied first to fees, interest or principal according to the admitted contract.
A model that stores only total debt can lose this allocation. Paying 5 might reduce principal
when it should settle interest, altering future coupon calculations. The resulting statements
may still balance, but the contract state becomes wrong. Multi-period replay exposes the error.
23.7
A payment allocation example
At a date, principal is 100, accrued interest 5 and fee arrears 2. A payment of 6 is made. Under
the stipulated order fees first, then interest, then principal, fees become zero, interest becomes 1
and principal remains 100.
Under principal-first allocation, principal becomes 94 while interest and fees remain 7. Total
obligation falls by 6 in both cases, but future interest and default status can differ. A total-debt
bridge cannot distinguish the two. The allocation rule is part of the contract.
If the rule is unknown, both allocation histories may remain possible. A scenario should
not choose the order that minimizes future cost unless management actually has that right.
Creditor-controlled allocation cannot be optimized by the borrower without permission in the
model.
23.8
Cure periods and clocks
A breach can start a cure period measured in business or calendar days. The state needs the
trigger date, deadline, required remedy and whether cure has occurred. Merely retaining a
Boolean breach flag loses the deadline.
Suppose a payment is missed on day 1 and can be cured within three calendar days, with the
deadline defined at the end of day 4 under the stipulated convention. A receipt on day 4 can
cure if settlement precedes the cutoff; one on day 5 cannot. An annual or monthly timestamp
cannot resolve this distinction.
The actual contract may count days differently, include notices or exclude holidays. The
simulator should not infer a convention from a generic date library. Exact boundary fixtures
should accompany the admitted timing rule.


PAGE 195
CHAPTER 23.
MEMORY ACROSS SEVERAL REPORTING PERIODS
173
23.9
Options survive quiet periods
An option can remain out of the money for many reporting periods and then become decisive
under a shock. A call right, put right, conversion feature or renewal option should not disappear
because it had zero exercise activity historically.
Two loans can have identical coupon and principal histories while one permits early redemption
at par and the other imposes a premium. A rate decline creates different refinancing choices.
Current book debt and past interest do not identify the option.
A state summary can omit the option only for a query where its exercise cannot affect outputs
over the horizon. That claim may rely on bounds showing the option never becomes relevant.
Once the scenario or horizon changes, the summary must be reconsidered.
23.10
A refinancing option value example
Suppose a company owes a fixed payment of 110 next year. It can refinance today at a cost of
102 if it has a call right, and current cash is sufficient. Ignoring discounting for this one-period
comparison, the right can save 8. Without the right, the old obligation remains.
If exercising requires a fee of 9, the saving disappears and the policy should not exercise under
a cost-minimizing objective. If the fee is uncertain between 0 and 12, the exercise decision is
conditional. A model that always redeems at par gives every borrower an option it may not
possess.
The example does not price the option probabilistically. It shows a state-contingent decision
consequence. Pricing would require a distribution or valuation operator over future rates and
policies, along with the contractual exercise schedule.
23.11
Estimates carry information history
An impairment estimate can depend on forecasts and discount rates available at the reporting
date. A later recovery in value does not mean the earlier estimate was necessarily an error. A
restatement that corrects a mistaken fact is different from an estimate revised because new
information arrived.
A historical simulation should preserve the information used for each estimate. If it recomputes
every past estimate using today’s knowledge, it changes the as-reported history and can
introduce look-ahead into validation. A current restated view can be useful, but should be
named separately.
The economic asset state and accounting estimate can therefore have different version histories.
A new estimate changes measurement without necessarily changing physical assets or cash. A
model should track the relation rather than force every valuation change into an economic
transaction.
23.12
Inventory age and obsolescence
Inventory with the same quantity and carrying value can have different remaining shelf life.
A demand slowdown can make one cohort obsolete while another remains saleable. A pooled
average age can conceal the threshold.


PAGE 196
CHAPTER 23.
MEMORY ACROSS SEVERAL REPORTING PERIODS
174
Suppose ten units expire next month and ten otherwise identical units expire next year. Both
pools are carried at 50. If no sales occur for two months, the first may lose all saleable value
under the stipulated physical rule while the second remains available. Historical cost equality
does not preserve the response.
A cohort model can retain quantities by expiry and location without individual serial numbers
when units within a cohort are interchangeable for the query. This is a justified aggregation
opportunity. If a recall affects a particular batch, the cohort key must include batch identity or
be refined.
23.13
Customer behavior has memory
A collection process can depend on invoice age, previous delinquency, dispute status and payment
promises. A single receivable total or average collection ratio may omit these predictors. Two
companies with equal receivables can have different next-month receipts.
A Markov model can be appropriate if the chosen state captures the relevant dependence under
its assumptions. Calling the process Markov does not make a deficient state sufficient. If
repayment depends on the duration of arrears, include age or a suitable duration state.
An empirical model should test whether the proposed summary predicts the relevant transitions
and whether stress changes that relation. A purely contractual due-date model answers a
different question from a behavioral collection model. Both can be retained with separate roles.
23.14
Horizon truncation
A policy can look safe over one year by issuing debt due just after the horizon. The closing cash
is positive, but the future obligation remains. A terminal report should show that maturity and
avoid treating its proceeds as permanent wealth.
Suppose a company borrows 20 at year-end and repays 22 one month later. A one-year cash
objective rewards the 20 inflow unless terminal debt is valued or constrained. A policy optimizer
can exploit this omission even if the event mechanics are correct.
Possible remedies include extending the horizon, adding a justified continuation value, requiring
terminal funding coverage or reporting residual obligations separately. Each changes the decision
problem in a stated way. No terminal rule is universally correct for all company questions.
23.15
A sufficient state is query-relative
For a one-day payment query, asset age may be irrelevant while settlement dates are essential.
For a five-year capital plan, vintages and replacement needs matter. For tax cash, legal loss
pools matter; for book equity, deferred tax measurement also matters.
The underlying representation should preserve these quantities where supported, while the
active execution state can be reduced for the query. A sufficiency argument names the allowed
scenarios, policies, outputs and horizon. It is not a permanent declaration that discarded details
never matter.
This is how compact simulation can coexist with rich historical evidence. The system need not
replay every old event at every step, but it must retain the memory that determines future
events and the provenance needed to reconstruct it after revisions.


PAGE 197
CHAPTER 23.
MEMORY ACROSS SEVERAL REPORTING PERIODS
175
23.16
Implementation tests across periods
A loss-pool test should distinguish cash tax from deferred tax measurement and preserve expiry
cohorts when required. A vintage test should produce different replacement paths from equal
carrying amounts. An arrears test should prevent double expense and apply payments in the
stipulated order.
A cure-clock test should distinguish receipts before and after the deadline. An option test should
preserve unexercised rights through quiet periods. A horizon test should retain obligations
beyond the final reporting date. Each case targets a specific missing state variable.
The tests should also verify legitimate aggregation. Identical loans with the same terms and
dates can sometimes be combined additively. Identical inventory units can be pooled by cohort.
The goal is sufficient memory, not maximal detail for its own sake.


PAGE 198
Chapter 24
Evaluating a real-company simulator:
what evidence would support its use
The exact cases in this part establish useful semantics, but a real-company simulator needs
a broader evaluation. It must represent disclosures, preserve important uncertainty, generate
coherent events and provide decision-relevant results. These objectives cannot be reduced to
one extraction score or one forecast error. This chapter proposes an evaluation design and
explains what each result would and would not establish. No empirical evaluation is claimed to
have been performed here.
24.1
Define the unit of evaluation
A unit can be a source assertion, a contract, a reporting period, a company history family, a
scenario query or a policy decision. Accuracy at one unit does not automatically transfer to
another. Correctly extracting a debt total does not establish that maturity and option terms
are represented.
A company-period case should identify its document package, entity perimeter, accounting
basis, evidence cutoff and materiality scope. A scenario case adds shocks, policies, information
and requested outputs. These definitions prevent evaluation from quietly changing the target
after seeing results.
The unit should also preserve unresolved content. If a system refuses an unsupported instrument,
that can be correct behavior within a bounded profile. The evaluation should distinguish honest
refusal from silent omission, while still recording the coverage limitation for the full mission.
24.2
Source-to-model traceability
For each material modeled assertion, a reviewer should be able to reach the source passage and
interpretation. Conversely, material source passages should have a disposition: represented,
irrelevant to the declared query, unresolved or outside the executable profile.
This bidirectional trace catches different failures. A model assertion without a source may be
an assumption or hallucination. A source passage without a disposition may be an omission. A
correct numeric total can coexist with a missed covenant note that changes the stress result.
The evaluation should sample across difficult formats and concepts, including scanned notes,
tables with overlapping dimensions, incorporated documents and revisions. A convenient set
176


PAGE 199
CHAPTER 24.
EVALUATING A REAL-COMPANY SIMULATOR
177
of clean tables is insufficient evidence for whole-filing coverage. The selection procedure and
omissions should be recorded.
24.3
Annotation and disagreement
Human annotations of financial meaning can disagree. A term may be ambiguous, a source
may conflict, or the accounting treatment may depend on facts not disclosed. The reference set
should permit unresolved or alternative annotations rather than force artificial unanimity.
Adjudication should record the competing interpretations and the evidence that resolves them.
A majority vote is not necessarily an authority on a technical accounting or contractual question.
For some cases the correct reference is a conditional family.
Ordinary comprehension review and expert technical review serve different purposes. A reader
can identify unclear exposition without certifying accounting treatment. An accountant can
assess a specific treatment without validating a probabilistic forecasting model. Evaluation
should not collapse these roles into a generic human-approved label.
24.4
Historical reconstruction checks
A reconstruction should reproduce observed statements within admitted precision and context.
It should also satisfy cross-statement bridges and contract constraints. These are necessary
checks, but a model explicitly fitted to the reports can pass them while selecting the wrong
hidden history.
Use withheld disclosures where possible. For example, reconstruct from primary statements and
selected notes, then test whether the retained family includes a contract detail revealed in a
withheld exhibit. The procedure must avoid using that detail indirectly through another source.
A family that excludes the revealed truth under correct source interpretation has failed coverage.
A family that includes everything can pass inclusion trivially but be uninformative. Evaluation
therefore needs both coverage and useful restriction, interpreted relative to the available evidence.
24.5
Synthetic truth and real uncertainty
Synthetic companies provide known complete histories and controlled disclosure maps. They
allow tests of whether the system preserves all intended alternatives and rejects impossible ones.
They are valuable for semantic coverage and adversarial construction.
Their limitation is that the generator defines the world. A system matching that generator
may fail on mechanisms the generator omits. Synthetic results should not be presented as
real-company empirical validity. The generator’s assumptions and diversity need review.
Real disclosures supply realistic ambiguity but often lack a fully observed ground truth. Evalua-
tion can then assess traceability, consistency, withheld facts, conditional predictions and decision
consequences. The two evidence sources are complementary rather than interchangeable.
24.6
Forecast origins and leakage
A forecast should be reconstructed using information available at its origin. Later restatements,
realized prices and revised macro data can leak into features or labels. The evidence store needs


PAGE 200
CHAPTER 24.
EVALUATING A REAL-COMPANY SIMULATOR
178
dated versions to prevent this.
A rolling-origin evaluation can produce forecasts at several historical dates and compare them
with subsequent observations. The model’s parameter estimation and tuning must also respect
the cutoff. Training on the full sample and then calling earlier predictions out of sample is
incorrect.
Company events such as acquisitions or changes of reporting basis can alter comparability. The
evaluation should bridge those changes or define a target that remains meaningful. A forecast
error caused by a perimeter mismatch is not the same as an economic prediction error.
24.7
Conditional scenario evaluation
A deterministic stress scenario may never occur exactly. Its mechanical correctness can still
be tested through contract formulas, accounting invariants and controlled cases. Empirical
validation of conditional responses requires suitable observed variation or another identification
strategy.
A model predicting cash under a stipulated 200-basis-point shock cannot be validated by
comparing it with actual cash after a period containing many other shocks without accounting
for those differences. Such a comparison may be descriptive, but it does not isolate the rate
response.
The evaluation should distinguish unconditional forecasting, conditional stress mechanics and
causal intervention claims. Each has a different comparator and evidence requirement. Calling
all three simulation accuracy obscures the target.
24.8
Probabilistic forecasts
If the system supplies a predictive distribution, evaluate both calibration and informativeness
with methods appropriate to the outcome. A forecast assigning broad mass everywhere may be
calibrated in a weak sense while offering little value. A sharp forecast can be badly miscalibrated.
Proper scoring rules, discussed in Gneiting and Raftery (2007), provide a principled way to
reward honest predictive distributions under their conditions. The chosen score should match
the outcome type and decision context. A score on annual earnings does not automatically
validate default probability or liquidity tails.
Probabilities conditional on one reconstructed history should be distinguished from probabilities
averaged across histories. If history weights are subjective or ambiguous, report sensitivity. A
good score under one prior does not establish that the prior is uniquely justified.
24.9
Set-valued forecasts
An unweighted family produces a set of possible outcomes. Evaluation can ask whether later
observed outcomes fall within it and how wide or structured the set is. A rectangular interval
display may obscure joint restrictions, so multivariate coverage should respect the actual feasible
set.
If observed cash and interest each lie within their marginal intervals but violate the modeled
relation C + I = 95, the joint model has failed for that observation. Marginal coverage alone
would miss it. Conversely, a wide marginal interval may coexist with a precise relation that is


PAGE 201
CHAPTER 24.
EVALUATING A REAL-COMPANY SIMULATOR
179
useful for a decision.
A set can be evaluated against a declared decision task: does it correctly identify policies that
are robustly feasible or expose the history driving a failure? This should supplement, not
replace, source and mathematical checks. A useful decision result can still rest on an incomplete
family.
24.10
Policy evaluation
A policy should be evaluated under the information and action constraints it would actually
face. A perfect-foresight benchmark can provide an upper bound in some settings, but it is
not an implementable comparator. A no-action baseline may be useful but can be too weak to
establish practical value.
A fair comparison includes a credible existing policy or classical method, with equivalent access
to data and tuning. If the proposed method receives a richer contract representation, separate
the benefit of representation from the benefit of its optimizer. Otherwise a solver improvement
claim may actually be a data improvement.
Policy value estimates need uncertainty analysis and attention to rare failures. A higher mean
payout can come with unacceptable liquidity risk. The primary criterion and vetoes should be
declared before selecting a winner. Descriptive advantages from a few simulations should not
be promoted to a default policy.
24.11
Ablations that answer real questions
An ablation can remove contract detail, actor information, persistent memory or joint constraints
to test their contribution. The comparison should preserve other conditions. Removing several
features at once can show that the bundle matters but not which mechanism explains the
change.
For example, compare a full debt schedule with an aggregate debt balance while holding pricing
and policy fixed. Then test the effect of adding endogenous refinancing. This sequence separates
contract-state sufficiency from behavioral response.
Expected failures can be informative. If the aggregate model misses a maturity wall, that
supports the need for timing in that query.
It does not prove that every query requires
instrument-level detail. The conclusion should match the demonstrated mechanism and scope.
24.12
Stress coverage
A validation set should include ordinary operations, near-boundary cases and severe but
admitted stresses.
Important dimensions include rates, currency, customer concentration,
maturity, collateral, offer capacity, settlement delay and information timing.
A Cartesian product of every dimension may be infeasible or economically incoherent. Use the
joint model to identify feasible combinations and targeted adversarial cases. Cover interactions
known to matter, such as collateral loss reducing funding exactly when cash falls.
Coverage should record mechanisms tested and regions untested. A large path count is not a
substitute. Ten thousand smooth scenarios can leave a single conversion or cure-period boundary
unchecked.


PAGE 202
CHAPTER 24.
EVALUATING A REAL-COMPANY SIMULATOR
180
24.13
Numerical reliability
For approximate methods, validation should compare against exact or highly controlled small
references, assess convergence or refinement, and inspect downstream decisions. Solver tolerances
should be translated into economic quantities where possible.
A residual below a threshold can still be unacceptable if it changes default classification. A
slightly larger residual in a large nonbinding balance may be immaterial. The evidence contract
should state which diagnostics veto use and which explain remaining error.
Repeat stochastic comparisons with appropriate seeds and uncertainty estimates. If candidates
remain statistically indistinguishable after validity screens, report that. A method can be viable
without being proven superior. Default promotion requires stronger evidence than an optional
experimental path.
24.14
Failure accounting
Evaluation must count unsupported, refused and numerically failed cases. Excluding them from
average error can make a fragile method look accurate on the easy subset. Report coverage and
conditional performance together.
A refusal can be correct if the request exceeds the admitted profile, but excessive refusal limits
usefulness. A numerical failure is different from a correctly identified economic shortfall. A
result table should preserve these categories rather than assign all failures the same placeholder
number.
If an approximation leaves probability mass unresolved, include that mass in risk interpretation.
If the model is unweighted, report unresolved regions and the bounds they permit. Renormalizing
completed cases is a different conditional target and should be labeled as such.
24.15
Reproducibility and independent checks
A reproducible run records source snapshots, model versions, assumptions, methods, seeds and
environment. It also preserves exact commands and outputs. Reproduction verifies that the
same computation can be obtained; it does not independently validate the model.
Independent checks should use different routes where feasible: algebra versus code, event-level
versus aggregate reconciliation, or one solver versus a certified reference. Shared data and code
can share errors. A second model reading the same incorrect assumption is not independent
economic evidence.
Rendered document review is also distinct. It can reveal missing symbols, broken references and
unreadable derivations. A clean build cannot establish that a reader understands the argument.
The manuscript should remain provisional until actual human comprehension evidence is
available.
24.16
A proposed staged evaluation
First establish exact semantics with the synthetic cases in this part. Then evaluate source-to-
model coverage on a bounded real disclosure package, preserving unresolved content. Next test
historical reconstruction against withheld facts and carefully defined reporting bridges.


PAGE 203
CHAPTER 24.
EVALUATING A REAL-COMPANY SIMULATOR
181
Only after those stages should a forecast or policy study claim economic performance. Its plan
must identify target companies, time cutoffs, baselines, tuning protocol, primary criterion and
uncertainty analysis. A failure at an earlier stage can invalidate interpretation of the later
results.
The stages are dependencies, not excuses to stop independent work. Source research, documen-
tation and bounded implementation can continue while empirical data or human review remain
pending. The full mission remains open until its specified evidence exists.
24.17
What a defensible conclusion sounds like
A strong narrow conclusion might state that a method reproduces all exact cash and ownership
transitions in a declared profile and preserves the tested information restrictions. Another might
state that a predictive distribution outperforms a named baseline under a predeclared score
with uncertainty evidence on a stated sample.
Neither conclusion should be expanded into universal accounting compliance or company
forecasting ability. A failed candidate can be rejected without rejecting the entire uncertainty-
preserving approach. A successful small model can justify the next experiment without becoming
a production default.
The evaluation program should make those distinctions easy to maintain. That is the practical
counterpart of the manuscript’s central idea: preserve what is known, preserve what remains
possible, and make every stronger conclusion earn its evidence.


PAGE 204
Chapter 25
Failures at the joins: how
individually plausible methods
produce a wrong simulation
Many serious errors occur where two reasonable methods meet. A contract engine can calculate
coupons correctly while an accounting adapter treats them as expense under an incompatible
basis. A particle filter can implement its weights correctly while a policy sees hidden future
state. A consolidation routine can balance while a liquidity engine spends cash in the wrong
entity. This chapter examines these joins and proposes checks that target the interaction rather
than one isolated module.
25.1
Reconstruction followed by valuation
Suppose historical debt carrying amount is 100 and an analyst reconstructs face as 100.
A valuation engine then prices the promise correctly. If the carrying amount included an
unamortized discount or accrued interest, the promise was misidentified before pricing began.
The valuation solver can return a precise answer to the wrong contract.
The interface should require a distinction among observed carrying amount, inferred face and
supplied valuation terms. If face is unknown, pricing should operate conditionally over its
feasible values or request a supported mapping. A test should include face 110, carrying amount
100 and proceeds different from both.
The repair is at the reconstruction boundary, not by adjusting the discount rate until price
matches book value. Such calibration would hide the missing contractual amount and could
distort every later scenario. A book-to-contract bridge should explain the difference with
admitted movements and measurement rules.
25.2
Valuation followed by cash forecasting
A bond’s market value falls after rates rise.
A cash forecast that treats the decline as a
cash inflow because the liability became cheaper creates money unless the company actually
repurchases or settles the debt. The valuation change is not itself a financing event.
If repurchase is allowed, it requires a market offer, available cash, quantity and settlement date.
The company may realize an economic benefit by paying less than face, but it still spends cash
182


PAGE 205
CHAPTER 25.
FAILURES AT THE JOINS BETWEEN METHODS
183
to do so. Accounting gain or loss depends on carrying amount and the applicable treatment.
A discriminating test changes market rates while holding contractual payments and policy fixed.
Cash should not change merely from revaluation unless a contract or collateral clause creates a
payment. A second test enables repurchase and checks the explicit transaction.
25.3
A hedge followed by accounting classification
An economic hedge can offset cash exposure while failing a particular hedge-accounting des-
ignation. Conversely, a qualifying designation does not guarantee perfect economic offset. A
simulator that equates the two can misstate both cash and reported income.
The contract layer should calculate the underlying and derivative cash flows separately, including
basis and timing. The accounting layer applies designation, effectiveness and measurement rules
under the admitted basis. Unknown designation affects reporting, while unknown notional or
reset terms affect economic exposure.
A test can use equal notionals but different reset dates. Annual net interest may appear small
while interim collateral or coupon cash differs. Another can hold economic cash fixed and vary
the accounting designation to show that reporting changes without inventing a new cash flow.
25.4
A credit loss followed by a funding rule
An impairment model may reduce receivable value after customer deterioration. A funding
model may continue using the old gross receivable as collateral. Each module can pass its own
local checks while the joined model overstates borrowing capacity.
The interface should distinguish accounting allowance, contractual eligibility and realized
collection. A borrowing base may exclude overdue invoices regardless of allowance, or apply
a separate haircut. These relations need explicit terms. The correct funding reduction is not
automatically equal to the accounting impairment.
Use a case where an invoice becomes ineligible before an accounting loss is recognized, and
another where an allowance changes but eligibility does not. The tests prevent accidental
identification of two different quantities. The repair is a shared event and state relation, not a
single reused field called net receivables.
25.5
A source update followed by a cached policy
A new note reveals a near-term maturity. The reconstruction updates debt timing, but a
cached financing policy was optimized for a later maturity. Reusing it can produce an infeasible
payment path while all displayed balances use the new source.
Cache invalidation must follow semantic dependencies. A policy depends on contract schedule,
offers, information, horizon and objective. A hash of the displayed statement totals is insufficient
because the totals may be unchanged.
A regression test changes only the maturity clause while preserving current balances. The
policy and relevant scenario result should be recomputed or marked stale. Unaffected historical
arithmetic may remain cached.
This tests precise invalidation rather than indiscriminate
recomputation.


PAGE 206
CHAPTER 25.
FAILURES AT THE JOINS BETWEEN METHODS
184
25.6
A restatement followed by backtesting
A current data pipeline supplies restated historical earnings to a forecast evaluation that claims
to use information available at the time. The forecasting code can be correct while the data
view leaks later knowledge.
The evaluation needs source versions and publication cutoffs, including parameter-estimation
inputs. A restatement can be used as a later target if that is the defined outcome, but it cannot
silently become an earlier predictor.
A test should run an old forecast before and after adding a later restatement. The frozen
as-published prediction remains identical, while a separately named restated analysis can change.
This is a direct check of evidence semantics at the validation boundary.
25.7
A particle filter followed by robust optimization
A particle approximation may retain only a small subset of plausible contract types. A robust
optimizer then treats those particles as the entire feasible family and reports a worst case. The
filter approximated a distribution; it did not prove that discarded histories were impossible.
The optimizer should receive the retained family for robust queries, or explicitly report a
sample-restricted worst case. Particle witnesses can supply an incumbent adverse case, while
symbolic constraints or other methods bound the unexplored region.
A test deliberately gives a low-weight but feasible history a severe cash outcome. Resampling
may remove it. The robust result should remain sensitive to it unless evidence excludes it. A
probabilistic expected-value result can weight it according to the admitted measure, but that is
a different target.
25.8
Scenario reduction followed by policy optimization
A reduced scenario set can preserve mean revenue while removing a signal pattern that changes
the optimal financing decision. Optimizing on the reduced set can then produce a policy that
fails on omitted paths.
Reduction should preserve or bound decision-relevant outcomes and information structure. A
representative scenario is not merely a nearby vector of shocks. Its observation timing, feasible
actions and threshold events matter.
A useful test has two close rate paths on opposite sides of a covenant and a refinancing action
available only before the breach is observed. A reduction that merges them must either retain
conditional behavior or report an approximation bound that covers the decision loss. Mean
matching alone is insufficient.
25.9
A policy optimizer followed by event execution
An optimizer can choose an issue amount based on continuous shares and immediate settlement.
The event engine enforces integer lots and a two-day settlement lag. The optimized plan is no
longer feasible. Rounding and delaying it after optimization can alter the objective materially.
The optimization problem should include the same action domain and timing as execution, or
explicitly account for a projection and its effect. A post hoc repair that borrows the residual


PAGE 207
CHAPTER 25.
FAILURES AT THE JOINS BETWEEN METHODS
185
cash is another policy and needs its own feasibility.
A test can use a funding need just above a lot boundary and a maturity before settlement.
It should reject the original plan or reoptimize under the correct constraints. Reporting the
optimizer’s objective before execution repair would overstate achieved value.
25.10
An accounting rule followed by an economic policy
A dividend policy based on book profit may distribute cash that is unavailable because profit
includes noncash gains or uncollected sales. The accounting calculation can be correct; the
policy is infeasible without a cash constraint.
Conversely, a cash-rich company can have accounting restrictions on distributions under the
applicable rules. A cash-only policy can violate those restrictions. The feasible action set should
combine cash, legal, contractual and class-right constraints where admitted.
A test should include positive profit with negative operating cash and another with positive
cash but a binding distribution restriction. Neither a profit ratio nor a cash balance alone
defines feasible dividends. The policy must receive the relevant state and rules jointly.
25.11
Consolidation followed by credit analysis
A consolidated group can eliminate an intercompany loan while a creditor’s claim remains
against one legal entity. A credit model using only consolidated net assets can assign support
that the creditor cannot access.
The credit analysis needs borrower, guarantor, security and transfer relationships. Consolidated
reporting remains useful but is not a legal recovery waterfall. The same assets can support
different claims under different entity structures.
A fixture should compare two groups with identical consolidated statements but different
guarantees or asset ownership. Recovery and payment capacity should differ where the stipulated
rights differ. If the model returns the same result, it has probably compressed away a material
legal-entity relation.
25.12
A default model followed by accounting close
A default indicator can trigger an accounting routine that zeros every liability and equity
balance. This makes the balance sheet close neatly while erasing continuing obligations, recovery
claims and operating assets.
The economic default event should create a process state. Accounting consequences follow
actual or stipulated modification, impairment, settlement and extinguishment events under the
basis. Default alone does not supply every measurement.
A test should leave unpaid debt outstanding during a cure period, then compare repayment,
waiver and restructuring paths. Another should include delayed recovery. The system should
preserve obligations until the admitted event changes them, even if a value function assigns
zero to shareholders.


PAGE 208
CHAPTER 25.
FAILURES AT THE JOINS BETWEEN METHODS
186
25.13
A pricing measure followed by a risk report
A debt price may be computed under a pricing measure, while a risk report asks for real-world
default frequency. Reusing pricing probabilities as forecasts confuses valuation with statistical
prediction.
The measure identity should travel with every expectation. A risk-neutral probability can be
useful for price consistency, but does not automatically describe actual event frequency. A
